HAVELLIN PALM BEACH

Operations Manual  ·  Internal Use Only  ·  v2026

1. Overview

This is the master document. It is the system of record for how the app is set up and how it works — configuration, engine, taxonomy, rates, gates. Its companion, the Concierge Job Playbook (concierge-guide.html), is the walk-through-it-in-order extract for running one job from intake to final invoice with the app open; it covers a subset of what is here, and where the two disagree this document is right. They are reconciled against the app together — the version stamp at the foot of each says when. Plain-text copies for a phone or a printer: MANUAL.md and CONCIERGE_GUIDE.md, hand-maintained copies of these two files (there is no converter), updated claim by claim in the same change.

Browser-based app used by all Havellin staff. No installation. Data syncs across devices (iMac ↔ iPad) via Google Sheets. Documents and media auto-upload to Google Drive. Some approvals require a manager PIN — see §17 for exactly which. Agreements go out for signature through DocuSign as of 2026-09-17 (§8a), and a client can pay the deposit by bank transfer as of 2026-09-18 (§8b) — that one payment records itself. Every other payment is still recorded by hand, and QuickBooks is not built (§3).

How we describe ourselves on client documents — corrected 2026-08-03. Havellin is insured and bonded. It is NOT licensed, and every client document said “Licensed, Insured & Bonded” until this correction — client estimate footer, invoice footer, both agreement footers, and the Terms line. Do not put “licensed” back on any client-facing surface. The estimate’s Terms bullet was cut on 2026-09-10 — it restated the footer seven lines below it on the same page, and Terms is for commercial rules rather than standing facts about the firm. The footers are unchanged and are where the claim is made. This is compliance, not wording: if it ever needs revisiting it needs Anthony, not a judgement call.

A third party's licence is a different thing and is correct where it appears — Licensed FFL on the firearms disposition list (§5f), the vendor directory's License / cert field (§13), and “licensed firearms transfer” in the collections cost note. Those describe vendors, not us.

The tagline on all four client documents is “Havellin handles the work no family should face alone.” It replaced Guiding Families Through Life's Transitions on 2026-08-03.

Navigation tabs (in order): on the left, the work on a client — Client Dashboard · Job Plan · Job Admin & Inv; on the right, the people we call — Contractors · Vendors · Referral Partners. The white space between the two groups is deliberate (2026-09-23, Anthony: “client facing tabs to the left, contact tabs to the right”).

⚠ Win / Loss left the navigation the same day, 2026-09-23. It is a row of four figures on the Client Dashboard now, directly under Total Jobs, and pressing Won or Lost lists the clients behind the number (§9). Anthony: “do we really need a standalone win-loss report or should we just fold that into the top of the client dashboard? … why don’t we just have those with expandable carets?” Nothing the tab showed was dropped — the four figures, the loss reasons and the lost prospects are all there, and the Won list is new.
⚠⚠ Client Intake and Build Estimate left the navigation on 2026-09-23. They are not gone — they are screens you open FROM the Client Dashboard. Anthony: “doing away with client intake tab and build estimate tabs. client dashboard needs a ‘Add New Client’ button at the top … same idea with ‘Build Estimate’. it should be a brown button above the job timeline.”
To…PressWhere it lands when you save
Create a client+ Add New Client, the first thing on the Client Dashboard’s client listThe new client’s own dashboard, at once, with Client created above the timeline
Build or edit the estimateBuild estimate — the brown button in the band above the client’s timeline (§9a). Once an estimate exists, ✎ Edit estimate beside the estimate document does the same, and un-approves it first if it was approved — until the signing packet goes out, when it is withdrawn and a change is a change order (§8). It is not offered while a manager has the estimate for approval, and comes back once they approve it or deny it.That client’s dashboard (as it has since 2026-09-13)
Both open as a full screen, the way a client opens from the list — not a pop-up. Across the top of each is a bar with the way back: ← Clients on the intake screen, ← the client’s name on the estimate screen. The Client Dashboard tab stays lit the whole time, because that is where you came from and where the bar returns you.

Going back without saving loses nothing while the page is open. What you typed on the intake screen is still on the form when you press + Add New Client again, exactly as it was when this was a tab. On Build Estimate, going back to the dashboard and pressing Build estimate again on the same client picks the walkthrough up where you left it — opening a different client’s estimate starts from their saved copy, as switching clients always did. Save is still what writes anything.

Build Estimate no longer has a job picker — it is always for the client you opened it from, named in the bar and on the Job card. The Clear button beside the old picker is now Start over: it asks first, then goes back to that client’s saved estimate if there is one, or clears the lot and starts again from their intake answers if not (the question says which, as of 2026-09-29), and changes nothing saved until you press Save. (Clear used to unbind the client as well, which on a screen with no picker would have left a form belonging to nobody.)

Nothing on either form changed — every field, gate and refusal in §4 and §5 is exactly as described there. Only the route in moved.
Three tabs were retired from the navigation on 2026-09-11 — Client Estimate, Agreement and Invoices. Everything they carried now lives on the job timeline inside the Client Dashboard (§9), on the row for the milestone it belongs to. Anthony’s reason for the change: “we are finding ourselves having to go to too many tabs … that way, as we’re going through a job, we know what to do next.” §7, §8 and §12 still describe those documents — what is in them, what the gates are, what each one promises a client. Only the route to them moved. Where one of those sections says “the tab”, read it as the matching row on the timeline.
Field mode keeps THREE of those six. The 📱 Field button in the header (or ?field=1) drops the nav to a fixed bottom bar of Clients · Job Plan · Vendors and sizes everything for a thumb. The other three are desk work and are hidden, never removed — the estimate still computes in full, and Save Estimate is on the phone (since 2026-09-30; below). Intake and Estimate were field tabs until 2026-09-23; in the field you now reach them the same way as at the desk — + Add New Client for a prospect who calls while you are out, and Build estimate on the client’s timeline for the walkthrough.

The Client Dashboard joined the list on 2026-09-11, because since the tab consolidation the timeline is where a job is actually run. Its buttons are live in field mode — field mode is a layout, not a permission level. The three things most worth doing standing in somebody's house are all on that timeline: recording a cheque just handed to you (the normal payment path on these jobs), recording a signature handed back, and marking the client won after the walkthrough conversation.

⚠ There is deliberately no field-only rule about which actions are allowed. The timeline's own gates already decide that — you cannot record a signature on an agreement that was never sent, on a phone or at a desk. A second field-mode opinion would be a second copy of that rule, and two copies drifting is the defect this app has hit most often. A test asserts the timeline's actions never consult field mode.

Entering field mode from a hidden tab lands you on the Client Dashboard — it was Build Estimate until that stopped being a tab, and the dashboard is where both the walkthrough and a new client now start. Entering it from the dashboard keeps you there, and so does entering it from the intake or estimate screen: the Clients tab is lit on both, so you stay on what you were doing.

The app opens on the Client Dashboard, the first tab. (Until 2026-09-23 Win / Loss sat in front of it and the app skipped over it on load.)

Interface notes: the header + navigation bar stay pinned to the top while you scroll; dialogs (e.g. Settings) scroll internally so their buttons are always reachable; and reference dropdowns (categories, dispositions, types, roles) are alphabetized — scale/pipeline lists (Condition, Priority, statuses, service tiers, Year) keep their meaningful order.

Phone & field use. Every tab and screen works on a phone — the nav becomes a scrolling strip, dialogs open as bottom sheets, and grids stack. The three directories (Contractors, Vendors, Referral Partners) are built for standing in a driveway: search, tap-to-call/text/email, and a Quick edit for contact details. Build Estimate is the deliberate exception — its room tables are desk/iPad work, and they scroll sideways rather than reflow.

What Build Estimate hides on a phone, and the rule behind it. Field mode (?field=1) keeps everything you can only learn by being in the house — room scoring, difficult access, multiple heirs, premium estate, collections, vehicles, materials, prep. It hides the commercial decisions and the arithmetic: crew sizing, the concierge hands-on share, the documentation scope, the timeline planner, discount, expedited delivery, fixed price, and every calculated total. Those wait for the desk.

The cut is observation vs. commercial decision, not "does it change the price" — every adjustment changes the price. Premium estate sat on the wrong side of that until 2026-08-03: the agreement (§3.3) defines it as a property containing fine art, antiques, collectibles or high-value contents, which is a judgment you make standing in the room and cannot make from a desk. Hiding it also achieved nothing, since the same flag is set at Client Intake, which field mode reaches from the Client Dashboard.

Nothing is ever removed on a phone — only hidden, and the estimate computes in full either way. Save Estimate is on the phone (since 2026-09-30). It sits at the foot of the screen with Reset, in a card of its own under the totals, so field mode no longer hides it with them; the message strip under it, where Save and the microphone print what they have to say (a room left unscored, a contract question unanswered), is on the phone too. It is the same button, the same save and the same refusals as at the desk, and it saves everything, the hidden parts included. The field banner says so: Save Estimate is at the foot of this screen and saves everything, the hidden parts included. Until you press it, what you have scored is kept on this device only. Until 2026-09-30 field mode hid the button and the strip with the totals, while the banner promised the estimate saved in full: a walkthrough scored on a phone stayed on that phone, unsaved, until Field · exit. Checked at 390 px and at iPad widths.

2. Initial Setup (one time per device)

Open Settings (gear icon) and enter:

FieldValue
Google Sheets Sync — Apps Script URLThe jobs / estimates web-app /exec URL
Google Drive — Jobs Folder ID1X2bmAAjbruL5lLip-UgwwmPNo7y_Ubrb
Vendor Directory — Apps Script URLThe Vendor Directory web-app /exec URL (separate sheet)
Referral Partners — Apps Script URLThe Referral Partners web-app /exec URL (separate sheet)
Gmail — Google OAuth Client IDAlready filled in. Change it only to point a device at a different Workspace; clearing it restores the firm default
Stripe needs nothing here. It runs through the same Apps Script as everything else, and its secret key lives in that script’s Script Properties as STRIPE_SECRET_KEY — never on a device. See §8b.
Creating the Gmail client ID — once for the firm, then pasted on each device. In the Google Cloud console, on a project owned by the Havellin Workspace: (1) enable the Gmail API; (2) configure the OAuth consent screen as Internal, so only havellinpalmbeach.com accounts can use it and Google does not require app verification; (3) create an OAuth client of type Web application and add the app’s own address as an Authorized JavaScript origin (the GitHub Pages origin — scheme and host only, no path); (4) paste the client ID into GMAIL_CLIENT_ID_DEFAULT in havellin.html.

This was done on 2026-09-08 and no device needs setting up. The firm's client id ships in the app as the default, so a new iPad works the moment it loads the page. The Settings field overrides it per device and clearing that field restores the default. It is not a secret — a browser client ID is public by design (it is in the page source of every site that uses one), and the authorized origin is what protects it.

⚠ THERE IS NO CLIENT SECRET AND THERE MUST NEVER BE ONE. Google issues one alongside the id; it is for server-side flows and initTokenClient does not take it. A secret pasted into this file would be readable by anyone who opens the page. A test asserts none is present. If one is ever exposed, reset it in the Cloud console — the app is unaffected either way.

The Audience setting decides what your team sees. On a project owned by the Havellin Workspace the consent screen is Internal and nothing else is required. On an External project, gmail.compose is one of Google's restricted scopes: production use needs Google review plus a paid third-party security assessment, and until then everyone gets a “Google hasn’t verified this app” warning they must click past. Testing mode (up to 100 named test users) works in the meantime — the token flow issues no refresh token, so the seven-day expiry that bites other apps does not apply here.

Two further groups in Settings are pricing policy, not device setup — they change what jobs cost and who can see the margin. Set them once, deliberately:

SettingDefaultWhat it does
Concierge production rate (α)50%The share of the concierge's on-site hours spent physically handling contents rather than directing. Drives hours, crew size, and duration — see Section 5i. Settings holds only the value a fresh estimate starts at (since 2026-09-02): the rate itself is set per estimate on Build Estimate, as Concierge hands-on share under the crew counts — lower on a trophy estate where the concierge mostly directs, higher on a straightforward Home Editing job.
Assumed Crew Cost — before anyone is named: concierge not yet assigned / outside concierge slot / specialist slot, standard / specialist slot, senior$100 · $60 · $30 · $35What we pay for a slot with no name on it; a named person’s own rate on the Contractors tab always wins. Feeds the margin panel only. Never billed, never shown to a client. See Section 16.
Changing either group applies to new estimates only. A saved estimate is pinned to the production rate and the crew cost it was priced under (the crew cost since 2026-09-02; an estimate saved before then reads the current Settings), so reopening an old quote on a device with a different setting never silently reprices it — the crew badge says so when the production rates differ.
Each directory (Vendors, Referral Partners) is its own Google Sheet with its own deployed Apps Script, kept walled off from job/estimate data. When you first deploy a script, set Who has access: Anyone and paste its /exec URL into Settings. A redeploy is Deploy → Manage deployments → edit → New version: the URL stays the same and nothing changes in Settings. New deployment mints a second URL that Settings does not point at, so never use it to redeploy. If a save shows a login/HTML-page error, that access setting or a stale URL is the cause.
The unsaved-changes chip, and what it is allowed to claim. A write that fails is queued, not lost, and a red chip sits bottom-left until it lands. Tap it — it opens and names the write, the sheet it was bound for, and the server's own error, with a Try again button. Only one wording is a claim about the deployment: Apps Script needs redeploying appears when the server answered Unknown type: <name>, which is proof the deployed script predates the app. ⚠ Until 2026-09-11 a bare Unknown action counted as the same proof and it is not — see the note below. A crash inside the script reads the Apps Script returned an error instead — held rather than retried, but no redeploy is prescribed, because a live bug on a current deployment throws exactly the same shape. Before 2026-09-01 both said "needs redeploying", which sent people to redeploy scripts nobody had touched.
⚠⚠ TWO PEOPLE ON ONE JOB PLAN USED TO DELETE EACH OTHER’S WORK (fixed 2026-09-11). Reported from a job: Ashley locked every room on a plan from the desk and none of it was on Anthony’s machine when he opened the same plan. The sheet merged a job plan as one record — newest save wins — so whoever saved last overwrote the whole of it: her eight locked rooms lost to his one changed checkbox, silently, with both of them believing it had synced. The Job Plan is the one screen two people genuinely work at the same time, one in the house and one at the desk, so it was the store that could least afford that rule. It now merges room by room, note by note, task by task. ⚠ Union alone would not have been enough, and it is worth knowing why: every device sends the whole plan, so the other machine’s copy carries a value for every room she locked — the stale one it loaded beforehand — and with only one timestamp per plan the stale room looks exactly as fresh as her real edit. Each key now records its own last write, and a key nobody has touched on this device loses to one that was. A plan written before this change is unaffected: with no stamps on either side the newer save still decides, exactly as it always did. ⚠ Requires an Apps Script redeploy (main-sync.gs, BACKEND_VERSION 2026-09-11b) — until it lands the app stamps correctly and the sheet still merges the old way. Anything locked on one device before the redeploy and not visible on the other should be re-checked and re-locked.
⚠⚠ “APPS SCRIPT NEEDS REDEPLOYING” OVER A SCRIPT THAT HAD JUST RUN (2026-09-11). Reported on saveAllJobPlans, with Unknown action as the reason. That string is emitted by the script’s doGet and by nothing else — doPost’s own fallback names the type, Unknown type: saveAllJobPlans — so a save answered with it never reached the half of the script that writes. Apps Script answers a POST to /exec with a 302, and a 302 on a POST is followed as a GET with the body dropped; normally that lands on Google’s echo URL and replays the stored answer, but when the request needs a Google session the hop re-enters the web app as a bare GET instead. The same hop landing on a sign-in page is the login/HTML error above, so the two are one cause wearing two faces, and in both the fix is the /exec URL in Settings and the deployment’s access — not a new version. The chip now reads check the Apps Script URL and its access and names the three things to look at: the URL is the jobs script rather than the Vendor or Referral Partners one, it ends in /exec and not /dev, and the deployment was published with Who has access: Anyone. ⚠ The text alone cannot settle it and what we SENT can, which is why the classifier is handed the request: ?action=createFolder and ?action=version are GETs the app makes on purpose, and Unknown action to one of those genuinely is an old deployment — a store write carries a type and no action, and there the same words mean the opposite thing.
⚠ A QUEUE THAT NEVER CLEARED, AND THE SENDING WAS THE CAUSE (2026-09-11). Reported from a job site as “2 unsaved changes — retrying…” that stayed lit, on saveAllJobPlans and saveAllJobs, both held on the login/HTML page above. That error genuinely is retryable, so the classification was right — but the retry fired every queued write at once, and every store write on the server takes a script lock that is global to the deployment. Two whole-store writes going out together made one of them wait on a lock we were holding ourselves, and a long enough wait is what failed it again: the queue fed itself. The retry now sends one write at a time, the way the outbound coalescer already did, and the two senders never overlap. Measured on the real page: 2 requests in flight before, 1 after. Three things follow. The chip counts attempts, and after six it stops saying retrying… and reads N changes not saved — still failing after N attempts, naming the two causes that never clear on their own — a stale /exec URL in Settings, or a deployment whose access is not Anyone. It says outright that until a write lands, that work is only on that device. And the browser now challenges you before the tab closes while anything is outstanding — which matters, because the queue is held in memory and a reload overwrites the local cache from the sheet, so an unsent edit is destroyed by the reload rather than merely delayed. Retrying never stops: the app cannot tell a wrong URL from a slow network, so it keeps trying and simply stops implying the next attempt will work.
A deleted client cannot come back from a stale device (2026-09-08). Every save from the app sends its whole client list, and the sheet merges by id so two devices can each add a client the other has not seen. Until this date that merge could not tell “a client this device added” from “a client the sheet deliberately no longer has” — so after Anthony cleared the Jobs sheet to start over, a laptop that still had the app open added one client and put three deleted ones straight back. The sheet now keeps a ledger of every job id it has ever held (a JobLedger tab; leave it alone). A job the ledger has seen and the sheet no longer holds was deleted — by the app’s Delete, by the reset script, or by deleting the row in the spreadsheet by hand — and a save carrying it is refused, not merged. The device that sent it is told which ids were refused and removes them itself, with a message: N clients deleted elsewhere — removed from this device too. That is the list catching up, not data loss. The estimate, job plan, hours and photo manifest for a deleted job are refused the same way. Consequences worth knowing: Settings → This Device → Clear is no longer the only defence after a reset (a stale device tidies itself on its next save or reload, though it will show the old clients until then); and a row deleted in the spreadsheet by mistake will not be restored by a device that still has it — run allowJobRestoreConfirm(['<id>']) in the Apps Script editor first, then save from that device. Requires the Apps Script redeploy of 2026-09-08.
What is in the sync sheet, and which tabs are dead (2026-09-09). Reported as “does the app write to all of the tabs in here? i feel like some are missing info always”. It does not, and two of them will never fill in. Written by the app: Jobs (one row per client) and the five JSON stores — EstimateStore, JobPlanStore, LogStore (the hours), MediaStore (the inventory manifest) and ChangeOrderStore — plus ContractorStore (the crew). JobLedger is written by the Apps Script itself. Dead: Estimates and Hours are left over from an older build and nothing has written to them for months; the real estimates are the JSON blob in EstimateStore column B and the real hours are in LogStore. An empty Estimates tab is not missing data. A store tab is only created the first time something is written to it, so a tab that is absent means that kind of record has never been saved — different from a tab that is present and empty, and only one of those is a reason to go looking.
⚠ REQUIRES AN APPS SCRIPT REDEPLOY — 2026-09-20b, and BOTH files. main-sync.gs (BACKEND_VERSION 2026-09-20b) and saveInventory.gs changed together, and they are one project and one deployment — paste both, then Deploy → Manage deployments → New version. What an older deployment does, and neither is loud: the client workbook gets no As-Found Record tab (the app sends the rows, the script does not know to write them), and a client folder minted by the legacy fallback path has no As-Found Record subfolder, so the as-found shots keep going to Estate Inventory. Nothing is lost either way and the next sync after the redeploy writes the tab.
⚠ REQUIRES AN APPS SCRIPT REDEPLOY — 2026-09-21b, saveInventory.gs. If you deployed 2026-09-21a, deploy again. One project and one deployment, so paste both files and Deploy → Manage deployments → New version; BACKEND_VERSION in main-sync.gs is bumped to 2026-09-21b because that constant is the only thing the app can read to tell which vintage is serving. Two changes landed on the same sheet on the same day and they are separate deployments. Before -a, a living client’s own workbook goes on printing Date of Death, Letters Issued and a §733.604 Inventory Deadline over three empty cells, plus an estate-only valuation block and flags. Before -b, a trust estate’s workbook goes on printing Letters Issued, a §733.604 Inventory Deadline and an Exempt §732.402 count on a matter with no court in it (§10a). Nothing is lost and nothing is wrong on an ordinary probate job — a payload missing either flag renders the full estate layout deliberately, so that an old browser tab left open cannot strip a court deadline off a live probate matter. Any living-client or trust workbook written before the redeploy should be re-synced: open the client on the Job Admin & Inv tab and make any edit, and the sheet is rewritten.
⚠ REQUIRES AN APPS SCRIPT REDEPLOY — 2026-09-22a, saveInventory.gs again. If you have not yet deployed 2026-09-21b, this one covers both. Same project, same deployment: paste both files and Deploy → Manage deployments → New version. Before it, an estate contracted at Contents list or None goes on printing Total Estimated FMV, an Items Awaiting Valuation count and an FMV BY CATEGORY rollup — a valuation on an engagement whose own agreement says counsel does the valuing (§10a). As with the other two flags, a payload missing this one renders the full valuation deliberately, so an old browser tab cannot strip the figures off an estate we really were paid to value. Any capture-tier workbook written before the redeploy should be re-synced the same way.
⚠ REQUIRES AN APPS SCRIPT REDEPLOY — 2026-09-30, main-sync.gs and saveInventory.gs (P11). One project, one deployment: paste both files from main, then Deploy → Manage deployments → edit → New version. Until it is done: (1) two people changing different parts of one client (a vendor line on one device, the team or the house checklist on the other) can still undo each other; (2) a damaged store tab would be emptied by the next save instead of refused; (3) a save that waits more than 20 seconds for the sheet’s lock would write without it, the interleaving the lock exists to stop; (4) Agent One can stop mid-answer on a busy photograph. After it: a damaged tab answers STORE_UNREADABLE and nothing is read from it or written to it (the app keeps the unsent save and retries; restore the tab from File → Version history in the spreadsheet); a lock timeout answers busy and the save is retried; Agent One’s answer limit is 16,000 tokens (was 4,096). The out-of-date banner now names this version, and it checks every action and save type the app uses — it used to check seven of 26.
⚠ REQUIRES AN APPS SCRIPT REDEPLOY — 2026-09-30b, main-sync.gs and saveInventory.gs (P16). If you deployed 2026-09-30, deploy again. Paste both files from main, then Deploy → Manage deployments → edit → New version. The app asks for this version, so every device shows the out-of-date banner until it is done. What it changes: (1) when the sheet merges a client, a payment keeps a void, a clear, the Stripe transfer it was matched to and its cheque photo from either device’s copy (§7, The payments list). Until the redeploy the older rule applies: a device that had not reloaded and then changed the same payment — marked it cleared, attached its photo — writes its older copy back, so a voided payment can come back and count again, with nothing on screen saying so. A device that merely saves something else is already safe. (2) The editor functions previewFolderDuplicates and dedupeFolderConfirm run from the Run menu with no argument (§7, re-filing). (3) When the sheet merges the inventory, an item keeps its serial, its recipient (Channel / Recipient), a firearm’s dealer route, its field note and the links between split lines from whichever copy has them, as the app already does: the sheet’s own list had fallen behind the app’s. Until the redeploy an older copy of an item, saved over a newer one, drops those on the sheet until the device that holds them next saves the inventory, and a firearm can fall back behind the transport gate. Nothing else the app calls has changed.
⚠ REQUIRES AN APPS SCRIPT REDEPLOY — 2026-10-01, main-sync.gs (P17). If you deployed 2026-09-30b, deploy again. Paste main-sync.gs from main, then Deploy → Manage deployments → edit → New version. The app asks for this version, so every device shows the out-of-date banner until it is done. What it changes: the executed agreement and its certificate of completion are filed into the Agreement folder by name, replacing a copy already there and keeping its link — the rule every other filing already follows. The new 📁 File signed copy (§8a) relies on it: until the redeploy, pressing it after a certificate that did not come back adds a second … - SIGNED beside the first (the editor’s previewFolderDuplicates / dedupeFolderConfirm collapse it). Nothing else the app calls has changed, and 2026-09-30b’s changes are in it.
⚠ REQUIRES AN APPS SCRIPT REDEPLOY — 2026-10-03, main-sync.gs and saveInventory.gs (P19). If you deployed 2026-10-01, deploy again. Paste both files from main, then Deploy → Manage deployments → edit → New version. The app asks for this version, so every device shows the out-of-date banner until it is done. What it changes: (1) when the sheet merges a client, the six lists P19 adds to the job (co-representatives and co-trustees, beneficiaries, specific bequests, the signed papers filed to Drive, finds on site, proceeds statements) merge entry by entry, as payments already do. Until the redeploy the whole newer copy of the client wins, so two devices adding to one of those lists can drop each other’s entry, and a void can be undone by a device that had not reloaded. (2) The client’s inventory workbook heads its net total for whoever holds the proceeds and finds the Net column under either name (§10a, the note on the Net column). Until the redeploy a trust’s workbook heads its total Net to Estate. Nothing else the app calls has changed, and 2026-10-01’s and 2026-09-30b’s changes are in it.
⚠ REQUIRES AN APPS SCRIPT REDEPLOY — 2026-10-05, main-sync.gs (P20). If you deployed 2026-10-03, deploy again; if you never deployed 2026-10-03, this one covers it. Paste both files from main (main-sync.gs changed; saveInventory.gs carries 2026-10-03’s change), then Deploy → Manage deployments → edit → New version. The app asks for this version, so every device shows the out-of-date banner until it is done. What it changes: every co-representative recorded on an estate signs the agreement in DocuSign beside the client (§8a). esignSend puts each one on the envelope at the client’s routing order, with a signature and a date on their own block of the signature page, and answers with who it put there; esignStatus finds the client by DocuSign’s recipient id rather than as the first signer, and reports each co-representative’s signature with its day. Until the redeploy the envelope still goes to the client and Anthony alone: the send’s notice says the co-representatives were not put on it and sign a printed copy, and once the client has signed, the Agreement signed row flags their signature as not on record until the page they signed is filed (§8). Nothing else the app calls has changed, and 2026-10-03’s, 2026-10-01’s and 2026-09-30b’s changes are in it: if 2026-10-03 never went live, what the note above says is broken (the six lists merged as whole records, a trust workbook headed Net to Estate) stays broken until this one is.
Records for a deleted client no longer pile up (2026-09-09). Deleting a client used to clear the Jobs row plus three of the five stores — the photo manifest and the change orders stayed behind forever — and a row deleted by hand in the spreadsheet cleared nothing at all. The sheet had accumulated five estimates against one job that way. Now: the app’s Delete purges all five stores, and every ordinary save sweeps out records whose client the ledger knows was deleted. Nothing is needed from you. For the leftovers that predate the ledger, run previewOrphanRecords() in the Apps Script editor to see them and pruneOrphanRecordsConfirm() to clear them — a one-off, since everything from 2026-09-09 is handled automatically. The automatic sweep never acts on an empty Jobs sheet, because with no clients present every stored record would look orphaned; clearing everything on purpose is still resetAllJobDataConfirm(). Requires the Apps Script redeploy of 2026-09-09.
⚠⚠ A RECORDED PAYMENT COULD BE DESTROYED BY AN UNRELATED EDIT ON THE OTHER DEVICE (fixed 2026-09-12). The same rule as the job plan above, one level up, and this one reaches money. The sheet merged a client as one record too — newest save wins — and a client carries several things two people genuinely change separately: the payments received, which documents have gone out and been filed, the appraiser roster and the inventory snapshots. Measured on the real sheet, both devices holding that morning’s copy: a $12,857 deposit cheque recorded at the desk at 2pm was gone by 3pm, when the house marked the agreement signed against its own morning copy — along with the record that the deposit invoice had ever been sent. Nothing on either screen said so. A client now merges payment by payment, document by document, with scalars like the status or a corrected name still following the newer save. ⚠ Removing an appraiser still propagates, because the app records the removal rather than simply leaving the record out — a record merely missing from a payload means “this device never saw it” and can never be read as a deletion. A snapshot is no longer removed at all (since 2026-10-03, P19): it is voided with a reason, and the void reaches every device the same way (§10a). ⚠ Requires the Apps Script redeploy of 2026-09-12 (main-sync.gs, BACKEND_VERSION 2026-09-12a). Prelaunch, so no real money was ever lost this way; if a payment recorded before the redeploy is not on the client, record it again.
⚠⚠ A DELETED HOURS ENTRY USED TO COME BACK (fixed 2026-09-12). Deleting a line from a job’s hours log never reached the sheet at all: the sheet merged the log by adding entries it had not seen and removing none, so the line came straight back to the same device on its next page load, and to every other device too. It bills the client, because the final invoice trues the labour charge to this log. Measured: delete a mis-typed 8-hour line from a 21-hour job, log tomorrow’s hours, and the job reads 26 hours. A deletion is now recorded as a deletion and survives a save from a device that still has the line. ⚠ Requires the same redeploy. Any hours entry deleted before 2026-09-12 is very likely still on the job — open the log and check the total before you invoice.
⚠⚠ A VENDOR, A QUOTE OR THE JOB TEAM SET ON THE JOB PLAN COULD BE UNDONE BY THE OTHER DEVICE (fixed 2026-09-29). The Vendors & partners fold and the team sign-off are saved on the client’s record, not on the plan, and until this date saving them did not move the client’s clock. Every save from either device sends every client, and when two copies carry the same clock the sheet keeps the one that arrived last — so the next save from a device that had loaded the client earlier in the day wrote that earlier copy back. An edit to a different client was enough. Measured with two browsers on the real sheet code: a confirmed $23,400 painter, the mover, an $850 dumpster and a confirmed team all came off the job, and the final invoice then charged the 30% prep fee on the $20,000 estimate figure ($6,000, not $7,020) and dropped the dumpster. Nothing on either screen said so. Every change a person makes to a client now moves its clock before it is saved, so an older copy can no longer win a tie. A few other client fields were saved the same way and are covered too — the probate package tick, the estimate figures on the client list, the approval flag the manager’s PIN sets, and a photograph attached to a recorded payment. Hours saves write nothing to the client unless they lock a newly named specialist. App-only, no redeploy. Any vendor, quote or team entered before 2026-09-29 on a client worked from two devices is worth re-checking on the Job Plan.

⚠ Both devices changing the same client — merged part by part from Apps Script 2026-09-30 (P11). On a current deployment the sheet merges a client key by key for everything two people edit at once: each vendor, prep, logistics and collection line; the team (concierge, second concierge, the specialists and the sign-off, each on its own); the vendor ratings; the review ask; and each row of the house checklist, as well as payments, documents and must-find ticks. So the house setting the painter and the desk (holding the morning’s copy) setting the dumpster both land. Only the plain client fields (address, dates, service and the like) still go to the newer copy as a whole. Until the redeploy the older rule applies and the later change takes the whole client. Two things are fixed in the app itself, no redeploy: a device that has not been reloaded no longer writes its older copy back just by opening a client whose agreement is out for DocuSign or whose payment link is out (a check that learns nothing new now claims nothing), and the Job Plan’s 20-second refresh no longer puts back a room, note or hour you changed while it was fetching. The refresh brings rooms, notes, ticks and hours — not vendors or the team, which live on the client — so reload before changing those on a client somebody else is working.
A must-find Found it tick merges per key from Apps Script 2026-09-19a — and the app works without the redeploy. The tick (§11) is written on the job record and stamped, the way a payment or a document send is. On a current deployment a tick made in the house survives an unrelated edit of the same client on a stale laptop, and an untick is a real removal. On an older one the tick merges the way the intake flags themselves always have — newer whole record wins — so a stale laptop can undo it and the tick has to be pressed again. Nothing else in main-sync.gs changed; redeploy when convenient, not urgently.

2a. Quick Links

Internal — do not print or forward outside the team. These open the underlying Google files; access is still gated by your Google sign-in, so a link alone grants nothing to someone without permission.

Jobs & estimates data — Google Sheet (manager)https://docs.google.com/spreadsheets/d/16Z3yiRYbhYLsia0aG4v5znWo_O2eDRnB0dcldECjAJM/edit

To open the Apps Script behind any of these sheets (to view, edit, or re-deploy the code): open the sheet, then Extensions → Apps Script. That's how you reach the main sync script (main-sync.gs + saveInventory.gs) and each directory's script.

The Apps Script /exec web-app URLs are deliberately not listed here — they're live API endpoints (effectively keys) and pasting one in a browser just returns raw data, not a page. They live only in the app's Settings; keep them there, not in a printable doc.

3. End-to-End Job Workflow

Intake → Walkthrough → Estimate → Manager Approval → Send to Client → Client Accepts (Mark Won)
→ Send the Signing Packet → Staff the Job Team → Signature Returns → Deposit Invoice → Record Deposit
→ Job Active → Job Plan / Document Property → Log Hours → Midpoint Invoice & Payment
→ Work Complete (Close job) → Final Invoice → Final Payment
Four points in this flow are hard gates — the app refuses, it does not merely warn.
  • Staffing requires the job to be marked Won, and so does the Job Plan itself — it does not open until the estimate is approved and the client has said yes. Never commit a specialist to a job the client hasn't accepted — holding someone for these dates is turning down other work — and building a plan for a job you haven't won is wasted effort.
  • Logging hours requires the confirmed job team and the deposit. The deposit gate has no override anywhere, and that is only safe because the deposit is never waived.
  • Activating the job requires the signed agreement and the deposit, and on a probate matter the Letters of Administration reading Received or Not Required (§4) — and, since 2026-10-03 (P19), on a trust-only matter the successor trustee’s Certification of Trust the same way.
  • A final invoice on an hourly job with no hours logged is refused outright (§12) — no PIN unlocks a missing timesheet. A fixed price never consults the log, and a fee-only Home Prep job has none.
⚠ DOCUSIGN IS BUILT AS OF 2026-09-17 — this note used to say it was not. On a job sent through DocuSign the signature comes back on its own: the app checks when you open the client, marks the agreement signed, names the person who actually signed it, and files the executed copy and its certificate of completion to Drive. You do not mark it signed by hand and the app will not let you — see §8a. Anthony has sent envelopes through it that worked (reported 2026-09-30).

⚠ AND A BANK TRANSFER NOW RECORDS ITSELF TOO, as of 2026-09-18. A client sent an ACH payment link pays from their bank account, and when the transfer settles the app writes the payment, names Stripe as the recorder and funds the job — see §8b. Cards are not accepted at all, deliberately, and since 2026-09-30 the payment recorder no longer offers Card either (§7). Not yet proven live: a test ACH payment is still owed (§8b).

Still not built, and still by hand: QuickBooks reconciliation, and every payment that does not come through that link — cheques, wires and cash are all recorded by you. And the paper route is deliberately kept: any single agreement can be sent as a PDF to sign by hand, per job, without changing a firm-wide setting.

Staffing sits in the waiting period on purpose. Once the agreement is out for signature there's usually a few days of dead time — that's when you confirm who is actually available and name the crew on the Job Plan. Save & Confirm Job Team is what unlocks hours logging, so a job can't reach its first working day with an unnamed crew (Section 11).

Home Prep for Sale follows a streamlined variant of this flow — no room scoring and no crew. Since 2026-09-14 it can carry declutter hours for the concierge; see Section 6.

4. New Client Intake

Open it with + Add New Client at the top of the Client Dashboard — it is a full screen off the dashboard, not a tab, as of 2026-09-23 (§1). Save Client refuses a blank required field and names it. The required fields are the ones marked * (they follow the service: an estate asks no phone or email of the deceased, and only Probate and Contested Probate require the attorney and the case number), How is this estate being administered? on all three estate services included — it carries the mark here as on Edit Client since 2026-09-30 (it was required and unmarked on this form before). The walkthrough date and who did the site visit are asked if booked (their asterisks promised a check the save never made, and the dashboard’s Set the walkthrough date step asks later; changed 2026-09-30). Job ID is auto-generated (HVL-YYMM-XXXX). Google Drive folders are created automatically on save — no manual step needed. Save Client lands you on the new client’s own dashboard, straight away, with Client created above the timeline — whose brown button is the next thing to do (usually Build estimate, or Set the walkthrough date if none was booked). It used to hop to the client list after a pause and tell you to find them there. A refusal keeps you on the form, naming what is missing.

The form is two columns, and which column a block sits in decides where it lands on a phone. Left is the standing facts — service type, the client or estate contact, the property address and the referral source, then the whole property block: type (with the concierge beside it), square footage and years in home, beds and baths, approximate value, the walkthrough, start and hard-target dates, priority, RE potential, premium estate, and the move destination on a Home Transition. Right is the conversation — the two What’s In The House questions and the tick list, Notes, then on a decedent job the authorized representative, the estate details, the estate attorney and the two documentation gates; the documentation level under them on every service but Home Prep; and the court record at the foot on a Probate service, or on any estate whose matter is Probate or Both.

⚠ Reflowed 2026-09-22, and the reason is the phone. The grid collapses to a single column under 820px and renders the left card whole before the right, so everything in the right-hand column lands below everything in the left. Measured on a probate intake at 390px before the change: property type at y=4,235 and square footage at y=4,388 — below the two questions at y=3,282 — so a concierge taking the call on a phone scrolled past the entire estate block, the attorney and the court record before reaching how big is the house. After: property type 1,020, square footage 1,173, walkthrough date 1,438. Nothing about what is saved changed, and no field moved between forms; only which card each block sits in.
Assigned Transition Concierge is optional at intake. Intake often runs a fortnight or more ahead of the work, and nobody knows who is free that far out. The field is still there if you already know — but the concierge is properly staffed on the Job Plan along with the rest of the crew, once the agreement is out for signature (Section 11).

Service Types, in the dropdown’s order: Home Prep for Sale · Home Editing · Home Transition · Home Cleanout · Estate Settlement · Probate Estate Settlement · Contested Probate Estate Settlement. On a matter recorded Trust or Neither the client’s documents name the last two Estate Settlement and Contested Estate Settlement (since 2026-10-05, P20; A Probate service on a matter with no court, below); every screen keeps the names above, because there the service is the price basis.

Renamed 2026-09-08. Downsizing is now Home Editing and Downsizing & Move Management is now Home Transition. Anthony’s reasoning: with no move to manage, all we are doing is editing what a couple takes to their new home. The names changed everywhere a service is printed — the intake dropdown, the reference bands (retired 2026-09-30; the Pricing Reference Check now prices the house itself, §5i), the client estimate’s fee-table sub-header, the dashboard, both filters, and the standard agreement’s Services and Service Management Fee clauses. The stored keys did not change, so nothing about pricing, stage copy or agreement routing moved, and a job saved under the old name shows the new one the next time it is drawn. The materials package tiers that were Downsizing Basic / Standard / Premium read Home Editing / Transition now; the Move Management card on Build Estimate keeps its name because it describes the move-day work inside a Home Transition, not the service.

Property Types: Apartment · Town Home · Patio Home · Single Family Home · Estate. (Property type is a label; square footage drives sizing.)

Home details: Square footage, Bedrooms, Full baths, and Half baths are captured separately (so "5 baths" is never ambiguous), plus years in home and approximate property value.

⚠⚠ Two answers here are OPTIONAL on this form and a HARD REFUSAL later, and until 2026-09-22 there was no way to supply either one afterwards. Approximate home value, on every labour job, and New home sq ft on a Home Transition. Both are accepted blank at intake — and Build Estimate then refuses to save the walkthrough without them. Both refusals used to read “set it in Client Intake for this client”, and Client Intake only creates clients: there was no route back into that form, no control in Edit Client, and no visible control anywhere in the app (the estimate’s own e-propval is a hidden input inside a display:none block). So a walkthrough on a client missing either was unsaveable, and the only way out was to re-create the client. Edit Client carries both now — the home value beside Property Type, the sq ft under its own Move Destination heading — and both refusals name it. Leaving them blank on the call is still allowed; they are simply worth asking for while you have the client on the phone.
The move destination is three fields, not six (2026-09-22). Address, city, and the new home’s square footage. Zip, bedrooms and bathrooms came off, because nothing in the app had ever read one of them: the destination prints as address + city on the Job Plan header and on the client estimate (both hardcode , FL), and the only destination figure that reaches the pricing engine is the square footage — it sizes move day, the supervision day and the unpack crew’s hours. A job saved before that date keeps the three dead keys on its record, harmlessly; nothing read them then either. Do not add a field back here without a reader — a test walks every destination key the save writes and fails if one has none.

All three decedent services — Estate Settlement, Probate and Contested Probate — require an authorized representative (executor, personal representative, administrator: name, role, phone, email) and a date of death. They are all offered the estate attorney, the What are we contracted to produce? question and the two documentation gates below. Probate adds the court record on top: case number, the date the Letters issued and the §733.604 deadline computed from it — and there the attorney and the case number become required. Property sale required? is asked wherever the court record is or the matter holds a trust (since 2026-10-03, P19; until then only the court record asked it, so a trust was never asked), in a cell of its own beside What are we contracted to produce?. An Estate Settlement whose matter is administered through probate (answered Probate or Both, below) is offered the same court record, with nothing in it required: answering the matter question shows it.

No Power of Attorney among the representative’s roles, since 2026-10-01 (Anthony). A power of attorney ends at the principal’s death, so it cannot act for an estate; intake and Edit Client no longer offer it. A record that already carries it keeps it: Edit Client shows it marked (as recorded), an untouched save writes it back unchanged, and the documents print the role as recorded. Change it to the role the person actually holds.
⚠ The representative block gained Letters of Administration on 2026-09-22, and before that the app only pretended to ask. saveIntake read an i-executor-auth the form has never rendered, so the ternary over it could only ever produce Pending — on the one field that blocks activation on a probate matter. It is a real control now, on all three decedent services, defaulting to Pending: Pending · Received · Not Required, built at load from one catalogue that Edit Client reads too, so the two forms cannot come to offer different answers to one question. Leave it Pending unless a certified copy is already in hand; it is corrected under Edit Client when it arrives, and the Job Plan’s Letters chip says so.

⚠ A blank is not an answer and neither form will store one. jobActivationBlockers blocks on === 'pending', so an empty string does not block — a probate job carrying one would activate with no Letters on file and nothing on any screen saying so. Both saves resolve an unrecognised or empty value to Pending, there is no blank option, and the reset sweep puts the control back to Pending rather than clearing it (a <select> ignores .value = '' and would otherwise leave the previous client’s answer standing). Since 2026-09-29 the activation gate reads the answer through the same rule, so a record carrying a blank from before is refused too, rather than waved through.
⚠⚠ THE COURT RECORD, THE LETTERS AND THE COURT CHIPS FOLLOW THE MATTER, NOT THE SERVICE (2026-09-29, workflow audit M5). An Estate Settlement administering a probate estate has a case number, Letters of Administration and a §733.604 clock exactly as a Probate job does — and until this date it had nowhere to record any of them: the case fields rendered on the Probate service only, Edit Client had no Letters date at all, and Activate job asked for the Letters on a Probate service only, so that job activated with no Letters on file. Now:
  • Client Intake and Edit Client show the court record whenever the matter is Probate or Both, on any estate service, and changing the matter shows or hides it on the spot. On an Estate Settlement a Trust or Neither matter is never offered one — there is no court case. On Probate and Contested Probate it shows on the probate track — a matter answered Probate or Both, or not yet answered. Since 2026-10-06 (P22) a Probate or Contested Probate service on a matter answered Trust or Neither is not shown the court record and is not required to give a case number or the attorney (it is off the probate track, the flag below); until then it always showed, and the save required both, for a court the matter does not have.
  • Edit Client carries the Letters date and recomputes the §733.604 deadline from it, so the day the certified copy arrives the deadline can be set where the job already is.
  • Activate job refuses on a probate matter, on any service, until the authorization reads Received or Not Required. A trust matter is never asked for the Letters; since 2026-10-03 (P19) it is asked for its own paper, the Certification of Trust (below).
  • The Job Plan’s Letters, §733.604 and attorney chips (§11) follow the same answer.
  • An unanswered matter falls back to the service, as it always has: a Probate service records that a case was open at intake. Nothing recorded before this date moves.
  • The desk’s documents print the case number only on a probate matter (since 2026-10-02, P18). A matter re-answered as Trust or Neither keeps the case number an earlier answer recorded, because the court record is no longer shown to clear it, and until this date every desk document — the Estate Inventory Report, the Contents List, the worklist, the release record — still headed itself with it, so a trust package would have carried a probate case to the trustee’s attorney. They print it only where the estate goes through probate.
What stays on the service: the asterisks. The save requires the case number and the attorney on a Probate or Contested Probate service on the probate track only (since 2026-10-06, P22; it required them on every Probate service), so on an Estate Settlement, and on a Probate service whose matter is a trust or neither, they are offered and marked optional.
⚠⚠ THE PAPER FOLLOWS THE MATTER, AND A TRUST HAS ONE TOO (2026-10-03, P19; Anthony: “i’m good with all of your calls. build it all”). A successor trustee proves their authority with a Certification of Trust (Fla. Stat. §736.1017) the way a personal representative does with the Letters, and since this date a trust job waits on it:
  • The authority control is named for the matter’s paper, on both forms, and changes as the matter question does: Letters of Administration on a probate matter (Probate or Both, or a Probate service whose matter is unanswered); Certification of Trust on a matter answered Trust, its hint citing §736.1017; and no control at all where the matter has no paper (Neither, or an Estate Settlement whose matter is not answered yet), so neither form names a paper the matter does not have. The answers are the same three, Pending · Received · Not Required, on the same stored field, so nothing recorded before moves.
  • Activate job refuses a trust job until the Certification reads Received or Not Required, with “The successor trustee’s Certification of Trust must be received” in the band, on the alert and as the client card’s red chip, Certification of Trust pending. A probate job’s chip reads Letters pending (it read Executor auth pending); its line in the band and on the alert is unchanged.
  • Trust Details: the name of the trust, the date of the trust instrument (Trust dated) and the day the successor trustee accepted, asked wherever the matter holds a trust (Trust, and Both, whose will pours into one), on both forms. Optional: the dashboard shows a blank as not recorded.
  • Property sale required? is asked on a trust as on a court matter (Anthony, 2026-10-02: “yes”), on both forms; the answer is the field it always was.
  • Co-representatives (Anthony: record every co-executor and co-trustee). Under the representative, + Add a co-representative adds a row: name, role (since 2026-10-06, P22, a co-representative’s own list — Co-Personal Representative, Co-Trustee or Other; it was the representative’s list, with Estate Attorney and Family Member and no co-trustee; a role recorded before reads (as recorded)), phone and email. A row left empty is no one; a row with anything typed needs a name, and both saves refuse it by number until it has one. The representative is never their own co-representative (since P22): a row naming them is refused by both saves, by name (… is the representative on this estate, so cannot also be a co-representative), one already on the record is named on the client card and after Edit Client’s save, and no envelope, signature line or release approval counts them twice. A co-representative recorded with no name (only another device could leave one) is drawn on Edit Client to be named or taken off, and the client card says so. Each one approves the releases with the representative and, since 2026-10-05 (P20), signs the agreement beside them (Anthony’s answer to Q22): each signs in DocuSign beside the client, which needs their email — a co-representative with none stops the DocuSign send, by name — or signs the printed page on the sign-by-hand route (§8a). The hint under the rows says so. (Until 2026-10-05 this said a DocuSign envelope went to the representative alone and that whether a co-representative signed the agreement was not decided.) A living client has none (the rows sit in the estate block, and neither save reads them off an estate).
Why the attorney is required on probate and merely offered on an Estate Settlement. An estate settlement frequently never opens probate at all — a trust administration or a small estate has no attorney of record to name — so the form offers the fields and the save accepts them blank, and the asterisks disappear to say so. It is still worth filling in whenever there is one: the Job Plan’s Before Day 1 stage carries an Estate attorney on file line on every documentation job, and until 2026-09-21 that line told you to fix it in Edit Client on a service where Edit Client had never offered the field.
⚠ The estate questions were asked on PROBATE ONLY until 2026-09-21, and an Estate Settlement was asked none of them. Date of death, Who builds the inventory?, both documentation gates and the estate attorney all sat in a block that renders on Probate and Contested Probate and on nothing else — so on the estate service Havellin runs most, the form simply never put them. The consequences were silent and all three were live: the job took the Full documentation default and was priced at full documentation, about a third of the ticket, whatever counsel had actually asked for; the 706 question went unanswered and unknown counts as yes, so it sat in permanent Strict Mode with the Documentation level dropdown disabled and the explanation rendering into a hidden div (measured on the old build: dropdown disabled, reason not visible); and it carried no date of death, which is the date every value on an estate schedule is stated at. They are all on the estate block now, on all three decedent services. Any estate settlement created before 2026-09-21 is worth reopening in Edit Client — fill in the date of death, answer the 706 question and How is this estate being administered?, and set What are we contracted to produce? to what the attorney actually said (Build Estimate will not save an estate estimate until the matter and the tier are both answered).
The service type is the living/deceased decision, and there is nothing else to set. Home Cleanout is a living owner clearing their own property — a landlord, a relocation, a hoarding clear-up. A house whose owner has died is an Estate Settlement. They are different engagements and they price differently (the estate services carry a documentation step and heavier disposition work), so picking the wrong one mis-prices the job as well as mis-addressing every document. If a client document has the wrong voice, correct the service type — there is no override, and the form deliberately never asks the question twice.
The service type is editable on Build Estimate, as of 2026-09-10 — but only within its own family, and only while the estimate is open for editing. That is before it is submitted, or after ✎ Edit estimate reopens it; since 2026-09-29 Edit estimate is withdrawn once the signing packet has gone out, so from then on a change is a change order (this note used to say until the agreement is signed). Intake is what you know off a phone call; the walkthrough is where it gets tested. Anthony’s case: a Home Prep client who turns out to need the closets edited as well. The picker on Build Estimate offers Home Editing · Home Transition · Home Cleanout · Home Prep for Sale on a living-client job, and Estate Settlement · Probate · Contested Probate on a decedent one — never the other family. A living job re-typed as an estate matter would have no authorized representative, no date of death, and a phone number belonging to someone who has died; those questions are only asked on the decedent half of the form. Crossing that line is a different client: + Add New Client. Edit Client used to allow it, keeping the wrong person’s phone, email or representative; since 2026-09-30 it refuses with Create a new client for this (below). Changing it rewrites the job, not just the estimate: the dashboard, the invoice header, the approval email and which agreement form the job gets all follow immediately, and the picker records what it was re-typed from. A service switched on Edit Client reaches an estimate open on Build Estimate for that client too (since 2026-10-06, P22): its picker follows, it reprices, and the note under the picker names what the saved copy was priced as. Until then the open build went on pricing, and saving, the old service.

Documentation level — Auto, Standard, or Formal — court & attorney grade. It sits in the right-hand column under the gate readout as of 2026-09-22, beside the engagement tier rather than down in Job Details where it read as a property fact. It is the second of the two documentation questions and it answers how strictly, not what we hand over. ⚠ Auto does NOT resolve by service type alone, and has not since 2026-08-24 — it resolves from the two documentation gates below and, since 2026-09-21, from the engagement tier, which is why that control is usually disabled with a reason beside it. Formal turns on the item-appraisal guardrails and makes chain-of-custody tracking mandatory. This is a documentation decision, kept separate from the Premium-estate rate toggle (§5c) — a premium job is not automatically a court-grade one, and vice-versa.

⚠ When the control is actually usable, measured on the real gate chain — because it looks dead and is not.
ServiceFresh intakeWith 706 answered No
Home Editing · Home Transition · Home Cleanoutusableusable (the question is never asked)
Home Prep for Salehidden (since 2026-09-22)hidden
Estate Settlement · Probateforced Formal, disabledusable
Contested Probateforced Formal, disabledstill forced — a contest is always court grade
The 706 question defaults to Unknown and unknown counts as yes, so every new estate job opens with this control greyed out. It frees up on an estate only when the 706 is answered No, no dispute is recorded, and the tier is not Inventory + appraisals. On Home Editing, Home Transition and Home Cleanout no gate ever fires, so it is always usable — and it is the only route to Formal there. On Home Prep it is hidden and saved blank: a prep job inventories nothing. That is what it is still for: Formal is what puts Chain of custody is mandatory on this job on the Job Plan header and turns the appraisal guardrail from an amber prompt into a red block. So it is not superfluous, although it is close to it — on the services where court & attorney grade means something it is nearly always decided for you, and on the services where you can still set it there is no court. If it is ever retired, those two behaviours go with it and there is no other switch for them.
Two things about this control were corrected on 2026-09-22. Its guidance read “Choose Formal when the job needs court-grade records — a contested estate, a large estate that may owe estate tax, or when the attorney or trust officer asks for it”, and all three of those now set the floor automatically and disable it — so the only guidance on the form named the three cases in which the control cannot be used. And the gates now borrow the control rather than overwriting it: forcing Formal remembers what was in the box, so answering the 706 No afterwards hands the blank back instead of leaving the forced Formal standing there and reporting it as a choice somebody made. It also repaints the readout when a person changes it, which it never did — before this, hand-setting Formal left the box directly beneath it still reading Standard documentation.

What we are contracted to produce — the engagement tier

New 2026-09-21, in the estate block on all three decedent services. It replaces the 2026-09-04 Who builds the inventory? question and it answers a different thing: that one asked how much of the work we do, which is a pricing setting, and nothing anywhere recorded what the client is told they will receive, which is a term of the agreement. Anthony: “we claim that our documentation is basically whatever scope they want us to do. But I don’t think there’s anywhere in the app to actually capture that … we have to know if we are doing a full documentation with valuation and that should be captured somewhere, probably at intake, certainly before we offer an estimate of cost.” It is one closed menu, picked at intake and correctable in Edit Client — a menu is quotable, and anything the estimator can type is something the agreement cannot promise.

TierWhat Havellin producesWhat counsel produces
Contents list
photographed, described and located; no values		A room-by-room list with a photograph, a description, a location and a condition for every item of consequence.Counsel values it, arranges any appraisal, and prepares the filing.
Inventory with values
estimated FMV, basis stated		The contents list plus an estimated fair market value at the valuation date, with the basis of each value stated.Counsel arranges any appraisal and prepares the filing.
Inventory + appraisals
specialist reports coordinated and attached		The valued inventory plus specialist appraisers engaged, scheduled and their reports attached to the record.Counsel prepares the filing.
None
counsel inventories		Sorting, staging and set-aside against counsel’s own schedule. No inventory is produced.Counsel’s office does the whole inventory and the filing.
Four tiers, three prices — and the 4→3 is deliberate. The tier is what the client is promised; the estimate’s Documentation scope (§5c) is its pricing projection, written onto the job alongside it so the twenty-odd readers of the scope did not have to be repointed. Contents list projects onto Capture only, None onto None, and both of the top two onto Full: they price identically, because since 2026-09-30 (Q20, three notes down) neither prices appraiser scheduling inside the document step — an appraiser is priced by its own vendor line, on any tier. Until then appraiser coordination sat inside that step’s coordination column and both tiers carried all of it. What they do not share is the documentation standard, below — and who arranges the appraisals, which is the tier’s answer (with, since 2026-09-30, the appraisers the estimate lists) and never the price’s (2026-09-24, two notes down). Do not collapse the two tiers.
⚠ Inventory + appraisals turns Strict Mode on, and it is not a decoration. It sets the Documentation level floor to Formal — which is exactly the machinery the tier is named after: the appraisal guardrail becomes a red block rather than an amber nudge, chain of custody becomes mandatory, the client estimate gains the court-grade records list, and the Court Inventory and the Trust Schedule hold at DRAFT until every flagged item is appraised or waived. The dropdown disables itself and the readout beside it names the tier, so it is never unexplained. Escalate-only is unbroken: the tier raises a floor and can never lower one a gate has already set, and when a 706 is also being filed the reason still names the 706 — the three reasons above it are facts about the matter, and a reader wants the fact they cannot change rather than the choice they can.
⚠⚠ The two top tiers differ on WHO ARRANGES THE APPRAISALS, and until 2026-09-24 the contract did not know it. Because both price at Full, and the estate agreement and the client estimate were reading the price rather than the tier, the two documents were word for word the same at both tiers — so on Inventory with values, where counsel engages the appraisers, the agreement’s §2 listed appraisal coordination for all asset categories, §5.2 promised that Havellin will coordinate professional appraisals … within the 60-day inventory deadline, §5.3 authorised Havellin to arrange professional appraisal, and the estimate’s records list promised independent appraisals attached. The Job Admin checklist and the Court Inventory already read the tier and said the opposite, so a signed contract and a court-facing schedule disagreed about one duty. They ask one question now (appraisalDuty), and since 2026-09-30 (Q20, next note) it has three answers where it used to have two. All — the tier is Inventory + appraisals and the estimate is priced at Full: Havellin arranges every appraisal. Listed — any other tier, with an appraiser line on the estimate: Havellin coordinates the appraisals listed in Exhibit A and counsel (or the trustee, or the client) arranges any other; the agreement’s scope of services says so in place of the carve-out below, §5.2 promises the listed ones (within the 60-day inventory deadline on a probate matter), §5.3 reads Arrange the appraisals listed in Exhibit A, and the estimate keeps the appraisals in its close-out and its records list. None — any other tier with no appraiser on the estimate: on Inventory with values §2 says in bold that the coordination of professional appraisals is not within the engagement and remains the client’s and counsel’s, §5.2 names who arranges them (the estate attorney, the trustee or their counsel, or the client, by matter type), §5.3 reads Admit an appraiser engaged by that same party (since 2026-09-30, P16; until then it read engaged by counsel on every matter, §8), and the estimate drops the appraisals from its close-out and its records list. The valued inventory is Havellin’s on both tiers. An estate with no tier recorded reads as Inventory with values here too, as it already did on the desk. Regenerate any values-tier agreement or estimate made before 2026-09-24 — the app was prelaunch, so nothing real was signed on the old wording.
⚠ Decided 2026-09-30 (Anthony, Q20): an appraiser is priced only when one is on the estimate. “If we need an appraiser, don’t we just add one? It’s not mandatory and shouldn’t be blocked either.” Until then the document step’s coordination column was what the Estimate Summary labelled Appraiser & inventory scheduling, and both top tiers carried all of it — about 5 concierge hours, roughly $750, measured then on a 3,500 sqft estate with six rooms — so Inventory with values priced appraiser scheduling that counsel does at that tier, and an estimate with an appraiser line on it paid for the appraiser’s scheduling twice. Now the column is Inventory scheduling, and full scope prices half of it (DOC_COORD_INVENTORY_SHARE, a starting figure to tune on real jobs, like the capture share); Contents list still prices none of it. An appraiser is priced by its own vendor line, added under Vendors on Build Estimate, on any tier: the touch model’s 4 touches, 2 concierge hours (§5d-i), with the appraiser’s fee pass-through like any vendor’s. Measured on the real engine on a 3,500 sq ft house with eight rooms, hourly: Estate Settlement $19,900 → $19,450, Probate $23,050 → $22,750, Contested Probate $31,200 → $30,150; the living services are unchanged. No tier requires or refuses an appraiser. At Inventory + appraisals with none on the estimate, the documentation scope hint on Build Estimate adds an amber line — “This tier promises the specialist appraisals: add each appraiser under Vendors, so its scheduling is priced on its own line.” — a flag, never a refusal.
A blank is allowed at intake; a job recorded before 2026-09-21 answers off its old setting. The tier has no default — on the first call the attorney genuinely may not have decided, and a guess printed onto an agreement is worse than a blank. It must be answered before the estimate can be saved — since 2026-09-22 Build Estimate refuses to save, submit or approve a quote until it is (see The estimate will not commit a price until both contract questions are answered below). It still prices while you walk the house; what it will not do is commit. Every existing job is migrated on read: Full shows as Inventory with values, Capture only as Contents list, None as None. Nothing had to be run on any device and nothing repriced. ⚠ Full deliberately does not migrate to Inventory + appraisals — that would have put every estate already on the books into Strict Mode overnight, silently, on jobs already priced and papered.
Intake seeds the scope; the estimate prices it. The estimate's own dropdown remains the thing that decides the price, so the walkthrough can override this answer — if the attorney changed their mind, or the paralegal turns out to be out of their depth. When the two disagree the hint under the estimate's dropdown says so, naming the tier rather than the scope — "The client record’s documentation tier is Contents list; this estimate is priced at Full." (it read Intake recorded … until 2026-10-06, P22, though the tier may have been set on Edit Client) — because the tier is the word the attorney was told, and Capture only is our pricing name for it, not a sentence anybody said on the call. Changing the tier later, on Edit Client (every estate service offers it), moves an estimate no manager has approved, and never one that is approved (since 2026-09-30). The estimate open on Build Estimate follows at once — its Documentation scope is re-seeded from the new tier and the price moves with it; Save Estimate keeps it — and the dashboard says so: The estimate open on Build Estimate follows it: repriced from Full to Capture only. A saved estimate follows the next time it is opened (it records the tier its scope was decided against, so a walkthrough override made against the tier the client still holds is kept), and until it has been opened and saved Submit for approval refuses it, and since 2026-10-06 (P22) so does the manager’s approval of one submitted before the tier changed (Cannot approve — the documentation tier on this client changed …, said before the PIN pad opens; deny it, and it reopens following the new tier), naming both tiers and the way in: ✎ Edit estimate beside the estimate on the dashboard, which opens a saved draft. An approved estimate, or one out for approval, is never repriced by the app: the save names the price it keeps and the route — ✎ Edit estimate until the signing packet goes out (it un-approves the estimate, which then reopens following the new tier), a change order after, and the manager’s approval or denial while it is out for approval. Until 2026-09-30 the tier seeded a fresh build only, so the walkthrough Save had refused for a blank tier came back from Edit Client still priced at the top of the scale. Two controls that both claimed to decide it would be the two-copies-drift bug again.
⚠⚠ The estimate will not commit a price until both contract questions are answered (new 2026-09-22). Build Estimate refuses to save, submit for approval or approve an estate quote while either the tier or the matter type is blank on the client. It does not refuse to price one: the rooms still score, the total still renders, and a notice above the fee panel says it is not a quote yet and why. That split is the whole design — the tier is a question the concierge asks the attorney while looking at what each answer costs, so gating the arithmetic would take away the one thing that makes it answerable. A refusal names the unanswered question, says to answer it on Edit Client, and says the walkthrough on screen is kept while you do.
⚠⚠ A blank tier was never a neutral answer — it was the most expensive one, taken silently. Measured on the real engine before the gate was built, on a 3,500 sqft probate estate with six rooms scored: an unanswered tier priced at $17,700 against $11,600 at None. $6,100 and 51 hours of documentation work, on a job where nobody had said we were doing any — and the agreement then promised the §733.604 inventory and the 60-day appraisal coordination in writing, on a contract the personal representative signs. (The appraisal half has narrowed since: the 60-day promise is written only on Inventory + appraisals, or for the appraisers an estimate lists — above.) The pricing fallback is deliberately unchanged: an unanswered job still prices at the top, which is what the notice warns about. What changed is that it can no longer be committed without somebody saying so.
⚠ And the record was answering the question for you. Until 2026-09-22 intake saved docScope: full beside a blank tier, and the tier reader migrates a missing answer back through the scope — so a job saved this morning with the question deliberately left blank read back as Inventory with values, indistinguishable from a job recorded before the field existed. Measured on the real intake form: every job the app had ever created came back answered, so the gate could not have fired on any of them. The mirror is blank when the tier is blank now. The migration still rescues a genuinely old job — one carrying docScope and no tier still reads Inventory with values — so nothing priced before today is blocked.
Two questions, two tests, and they stay separate. The matter type is asked of every decedent job; the tier is asked of every service that prices a documentation step. Those select the same three services today and they are not the same question, so each has its own predicate — a living-client job is asked neither, and is never gated.
