Once the job has started it is measured from the day it started; a date before the start itself reads The hard target is before the start.
There is no “ahead of schedule” flag, and that is deliberate. A job at 100% on working day 4 of 6 is good news, and the percentage is already printed two inches away beside the day count. Flagging it would be a line explaining what the reader can already see — which is the standing rule this app cuts every time.
Pace and fit are two separate slots and never collapse into one. Pace is how the job is running; fit is whether the plan reaches the date we committed to. A brand-new job with a tight closing date is perfectly on pace and its plan still does not fit, and one line holding both would hide whichever it did not win.
Steps on the rail carry their PLANNED date until they have a real one. A planned date always has the word Planned in front of it and sits in its own slot, and it disappears the instant the step has its actual. Everything else on the rail is something that happened; a projection sitting in that slot would be indistinguishable from a record, on the one surface whose whole contract is what has happened.

9a-ii. The band above the timeline — the step, and the document in play

The tan band opens with NEXT and the step. Under that sits the document tray — the document this stage of the job is about — and then one row of buttons: the tray’s own outline buttons first — ↗ Open the … draft while one is waiting, 👁 View and 🖨 Print (each naming its document: View estimate, Print packet), the 📁 Drive copy, and ✎ Edit estimate on the estimate — then the one filled button that is the thing to do, then anything else the step offers. Anthony asked for that order and it is the real reading order: you consult the document, then you act.

⚠ NEXT names the step to take, never the milestone it finishes (2026-09-23). The band used to print the milestone’s own name, so a job waiting on its midpoint invoice read NEXT · Midpoint invoice sent, which reads as though it had already happened. Anthony: “it just feels a little misleading … it makes it seem like we’ve already done that.” Each of the sixteen milestones now carries its step in the imperative and the band prints that: Send the midpoint invoice, Collect the deposit, Get the agreement signed, Activate the job. The rail below keeps the milestone names, because there they are the record of what has happened. A blocked step is named the same way (⚠ Blocked · Get the estimate approved).
⚠ ONE ROW OF BUTTONS, NOT TWO (2026-09-20). The tray’s buttons and the step’s used to be two separate rows, so a band offering four of them — View final invoice, Print final invoice, Send final invoice and Manager approval — rendered two over two on a card wide enough for twelve. Anthony: “let’s make the four … move horizontally rather than stacked two over two to save a little vertical scrolling.” Nothing was wrapping — they were two containers, so no width would ever have fixed it. They are one row now, in the same order, and it still wraps onto a second line on a phone. The one-filled-button rule is unchanged: sharing a row with the tray is what makes the filled button the one thing in the row the eye lands on.

The tray advances with the job — Client Estimate through the approval and send steps, then the Signing Packet, then each Invoice in turn. Twelve of the sixteen milestones name a document; four do not (intake, walkthrough, job active, work complete), and those are the steps where the thing to do is record something rather than read something.

⚠⚠ WHAT THE TRAY OFFERS IS GATED, AND BEFORE 2026-09-13 IT WAS NOT. Four of the five document rows offered their View and Print links on no condition at all. On a job at status New with an estimate built but not approved, not won and no agreement, the strip at the foot rendered View deposit invoice and View midpoint invoice — and those opened a complete, printable billing document priced off the unapproved draft. One gate now answers both what is offered and what happens when it is pressed, so the two cannot drift apart. If a document is not ready, its buttons are not there. Two refusals still come on the press, because only the invoice itself can work them out: a final outside ±15% offers View, Print and Send and refuses them until 🔑 Manager approval is given, and an hourly final with no hours logged keeps ✉ Send final invoice, which names the reason when pressed.
A DRAFT estimate can be read and cannot be printed. The tray titles it Client Estimate — DRAFT and offers View and Edit but no Print. A draft may be read on our own screen and must not reach paper; the word DRAFT in the title is the whole explanation, and a Print button that alerts a refusal back at you would be worse than no button.
Exactly one filled button in the band. The tray’s controls are bronze outlines; the step primary is the only solid one, and that holds now they share a row — it is what makes the filled button the one thing in the row the eye lands on. A second filled button would make the reader choose between two primaries, which costs the band its whole meaning.
StatusMeaning
NewIntake complete; the estimate is not yet submitted (a saved estimate, and one a manager denied, read New)
Pending ApprovalEstimate submitted for manager PIN approval
Approved — Awaiting ClientWe've approved our own estimate. The client has not answered. Nothing may be staffed yet.
WonThe client has accepted. Staffing unlocks here.
Won · Pending Re-approvalThe client has said yes and a revised price — a discount, or an edit resubmitted before the packet went out — is waiting on a manager PIN. Still won: staffing, the Job Plan and Win / Loss all read it as won. It is on the Pending Approval filter, and the PIN or a deny returns it to Won — or, when the PIN approves a price above the one the client accepted, to Won · Awaiting Re-acceptance.
Won · Awaiting Re-acceptanceThe client said yes, and a manager has since approved the estimate at a higher price. Still won — staffing and the Job Plan are untouched — but the signing packet, the deposit invoice and the Stripe link wait until the client accepts the new figure (§9a). It returns to Won once Client accepted the revised price is recorded.
ActiveSigned + deposit received — work in progress
ClosedThe work is handed over. Delivery is stamped here — see below. The final invoice goes out after the close and may still be unpaid; the timeline says so. Until that final goes out, Re-open job puts it back to Active (below).
LostDied before any money arrived. Terminal.
Closed — Deposit RetainedDied after money came in — any payment, part of a deposit included (since 2026-09-29). On a fixed price we keep what was received; on an hourly job we keep the deposit or the work done, whichever is more, and the rest is a refund due, recorded on the card when it is sent (since 2026-10-01). It still counts as won because it produced revenue. Terminal.
⚠ Re-open undoes a close, until the final invoice goes out (built 2026-09-29). Anthony: “add Re-open.” A close made by mistake used to need Anthony to fix it. This note said, the same morning, there is no Re-open, and there was never a working one: the client list built a Status button reading Re-open and the very next line of code replaced it with Open →, so it never reached the screen. There is a real one now.
  • Where it is: ↺ Re-open job, an outline button beside the lit step of a closed job, in the tan band — on the Client Dashboard and on the band at the top of the Job Plan and Job Admin & Inv. It sits where Close job was; the filled button is still Send final invoice, because on a job that really is finished that is the next thing to do.
  • It asks first. Re-open this job? names the day it was closed and the name on that close (the job’s assigned concierge, since 2026-09-30), says the handover date is cleared and the timeline goes back to the step the job was on, and that nothing else changes — the hours, the payments and anything already sent stay as they are. Cancel changes nothing.
  • OK puts the job back to Active and lands you on its Job Plan. Close job is back beside the lit step, Work complete reads Re-opened — the earlier close was undone, the midpoint rows are ordinary rows again, and the ✕ (mark lost) is back on the client’s row. The next close stamps its own day as the handover.
  • The earlier close is kept on the record — the day it was closed, the name on that close, and when it was re-opened, recorded under the name of the manager who approved the price rather than whoever pressed the button (the app has no sign-in). The handover date is cleared, never edited: a re-open undoes the close outright, so nothing ever moves a handover date to another day.
  • ⚠ A final invoice drafted but not sent is voided. After a close the band’s filled button is Send final invoice, so a mistaken close has often drafted one, and it billed the job as it stood at the close. The question names that draft, with the day it was made and the Gmail account it is in, and says to delete it in Gmail — the app cannot reach your mailbox to do it. (A plain email the app opened for you instead is to be discarded if it never went.) A Drive copy filed with it is replaced when the final next goes out.
  • ⚠ And the Final invoice sent row goes on naming that draft until a final is actually sent — “The Gmail draft from Sep 30 (anthony@…) was made before the job was re-opened — delete it, don’t send it”. Close again and draft a fresh final, and the row says to send the new one and delete the older, so two finals in one mailbox are never left for somebody to tell apart. It is the same record the price-change drafts use (§9b).
  • ⚠ Not offered once the final invoice has gone out, or once a final payment is recorded. The client then holds a bill that says the job is finished, and a re-open would put the app out of step with it. The button simply is not there.
  • A job closed before 2026-07-30 carries no handover date at all, and its timeline used to offer Activate job as the way back into it. It offers Re-open job instead, through the same question.
  • Lost and Closed — Deposit Retained stay terminal. If a lost client comes back, start a new job.
The handover date is recorded at the close, and nothing edits it. Closing a job stamps the delivery date, the time and a name — it records when the work was actually handed over, not the last time somebody touched a button. The name is the job’s assigned concierge (since 2026-09-30, Anthony’s call), and activating stamps the start the same way; until then both recorded the manager who approved the price, which is still what they record on a job with no concierge assigned. The one thing that clears it is Re-open, which undoes the close outright and keeps a record of it (above); the next close records its own day. Nothing in the app previously recorded delivery at all (the completion date on the intake form is a target, not an observation), and this is the field revenue recognition will read once the QuickBooks twin goes live.
"Approved" and "Won" are different things, and the difference is the point. Approved means a manager signed off on our own figure. Won means the client said yes. Previously the app had no way to tell them apart — it counted a job as won the moment we approved our own estimate, so the win rate measured how often we approved our own work.
⚠ A won client re-priced used to read as though they had never said yes (fixed 2026-09-29). Offering a discount on a won job wrote Pending Approval over Won; the manager’s PIN then wrote Approved — Awaiting Client, and a deny wrote New — while the timeline, staffing and Win / Loss went on reading the job as won, because the client’s yes is kept on its own. Anthony: “if we are offering a discount, and therefore it is ‘pending’ how is it also ‘won’?” A won job now reads Won · Pending Re-approval while the revised price waits on the manager and Won otherwise — or Won · Awaiting Re-acceptance after an approved raise the client has not yet accepted (§9a) — an estimate change never moves an active or closed job, and a won job that read New or Approved before today reads Won with nothing to re-do. A client who has not said yes reads exactly as before.

9b. The five client documents — one way to view, print, send and file

Client estimate · signing packet · deposit invoice · midpoint invoice · final invoice. Every one of them is reached the same way — from the tray in the band while it is the document in play (§9a-ii), and from the strip at the foot of the timeline whenever else it is ready, after its stage or ahead of it (§9a) — and the sameness is the deliverable — Anthony: “if they’ve sent an estimate they’ll know how to send an agreement, and they’ll know how to send an invoice.”

ButtonWhat it does
👁 ViewOpens the document in a reader over the dashboard. Closing it drops the document.
🖨 PrintPrint / Save as PDF. The PDF is named for the document, the street and the day (Havellin Service Estimate - 123 Ocean Blvd - Sep 30 2026), never a database key.
✉ Send…Builds the PDF, creates a draft in your own Gmail with it attached and the right department CC’d, and opens it. The signing packet goes to the client through DocuSign instead (§8a), unless you press Send as PDF to sign by hand.
✓ I’ve sent itAppears after a Gmail draft is made, or after the app opens a plain email instead (since 2026-09-30) — never after a DocuSign send, which records itself. Press it once you have actually sent the mail. Withdrawn if the price changes after the draft was made — a discount or Edit estimate leaves that draft at the old price, and the row names it for you to delete instead.
↗ Open the … draftRe-opens the draft you made. It is withdrawn once you confirm the send, and when a price change leaves the draft out of date.
📁 Filed estimate / 📁 File estimate to Drive (and packet, deposit invoice…)Opens the copy in the client’s Drive folder, or files it if that did not land. Each names its document (since 2026-09-30; they all read Filed copy, four in a row in the strip with nothing saying which was which). 📁 File … to Drive is not offered while the document is view-only — an estimate back with the manager after a discount or an edit — because that press is refused; it comes back once the estimate is approved again. A copy already filed stays offered: opening it changes nothing. ✎ Edit estimate takes the estimate’s link away with its approval (since 2026-09-30; it went on opening the pre-edit copy); it comes back when the edited estimate is filed.
Why there are two taps and not one. The app is allowed to create a Gmail draft and is deliberately not allowed to send one — that is the feature: every client email is read by a person before it goes. So between the app making the draft and the mail leaving there is a real gap the app cannot see across. Pressing Send records a draft; pressing ✓ I’ve sent it records the send. Recording the draft as a send would turn the timeline green over an untouched draft sitting in a mailbox — and on the estimate, that is what unlocks Mark Won. The row says “Drafted — read it, send it, then confirm” while it is waiting (or “Opened as a plain email — send it from your mail app, then confirm” when Gmail was not used).
⚠ A DRAFT THE PRICE HAS MOVED PAST IS NOT THE DRAFT ANY MORE (2026-09-29). Nothing reaches back into a Gmail draft once it is made, so a discount or an Edit estimate between the draft and the send leaves it at the old price. Until today the row went on offering ✓ I’ve sent it over it, and one tap recorded the old-price document as sent. Now the tap and the link to that draft go, Send comes back, and the row names the draft to delete — “The Gmail draft from Sep 29 (anthony@…) has the old price — delete it, don’t send it”, or “was made before the estimate was edited” after an edit, or “was made before the job was re-opened” on a final a Re-open retired (§9). A fresh send tells the two drafts apart until the new one is confirmed. The app never deletes anything in Gmail; the details are in §8.
A message on the dashboard stays until you do something (2026-09-29). The app refreshes the dashboard on its own — when the client list or the estimates finish loading, while it checks for the manager’s decision, and when DocuSign or Stripe answers — and until today each of those refreshes wiped whatever message was above the timeline. Submitting an estimate starts the manager-approval check at once, so “Submitted for manager approval” and the discount’s own confirmation both vanished a second after they appeared. A refresh the app makes on its own keeps the message now; the next thing you press or open replaces it.
The confirming tap belongs to the mail provider, not to the document. DocuSign sends on its own, so since 2026-09-17 a signing packet sent through it has no tap; the estimate, the three invoices and a packet sent as a PDF go as Gmail drafts and keep it. Nothing on screen needed changing for that.
The invoice had no real email at all until 2026-09-11. It had a plain-text message whose body read “Please find attached your invoice” with nothing attached — a plain mail link cannot carry an attachment. Any invoice emailed before that date went out saying an attachment was there when it was not.
Filing is answerable for all five now. Each document records when it was filed and where, so “is this in Drive?” has an answer on the row. A document that was sent and never filed offers 📁 File … to Drive, naming the document (File estimate to Drive) — that is the case that matters, because it is the one the firm could not produce later. A document is filed on its own as soon as it leaves our hands — the estimate when a manager approves it, the packet on its first print or send, every document the moment you press Send — so a draft waiting on I’ve sent it usually already reads 📁 Filed …; 📁 File … to Drive appears only when a document was sent and its filing never landed. (Corrected 2026-09-30: this note used to say an unsent draft offers neither, but pressing Send files the document the moment the draft is made.) Nor is the button offered on an estimate back with the manager (2026-09-30): the estimate’s own gate refuses to file an unapproved estimate, so the button waits for the approval rather than being offered and refused.
Fixed 2026-09-11, and worth knowing if you filed anything before then: the estimate and the agreement were filed to Drive by reading whatever the old Client Estimate / Agreement tab happened to be showing. Filed from the dashboard with another client loaded, that wrote the other client’s document into this client’s estate folder, under this client’s job id — and reported success. If a filed document in a Drive folder names the wrong client, this is why. Re-file it from the timeline and delete the stray.

Marking a job Won

Once the approved estimate has gone to the client and you have pressed ✓ I’ve sent it, the band’s filled button is ✓ Client accepted — mark won, with Mark lost beside it. Acceptance is informal — nothing is signed until the agreement — so the app records how you know:

  • Method — email reply · phone call · text · in person.
  • Date they accepted.
  • What they said — paste the email, or note what was said on the call.

A phone call or in-person acceptance with no note is challenged before it's accepted. For those there is no email to fall back on: the note is the only thing recording what the client actually agreed to.

Closing out a job that dies

The ✕ at the end of a client’s row on the client list opens closeout (so does Mark lost beside Client accepted). What it does depends on whether any money has arrived:

  • Nothing received → Lost, with a reason. A job that was marked Won and then withdraws flips back to not-won — a win that produced nothing isn't a win.
  • Anything received, on any stage → the dialog names the amount and the status becomes Closed — Deposit Retained. It still counts as won, the Win / Loss Won list shows the amount kept rather than the quote, and the job’s timeline collapses to one row naming it. On a fixed price the button reads Close — Retain $2,000 (whatever was received): the stage earn-out is unchanged.
  • On an hourly job (Home Prep included), since 2026-10-01 → the dialog also works out what the job has earned and what is due back (Anthony: “refund the excess. Keep the deposit plus the work done (hours and materials)”). Three rows: Received; Earned: the deposit ($X) or the work done ($Y), whichever is more; and Refund due, what was received less what was earned, never below zero. The work done is what the final invoice bills — the hours logged at the job’s rates, with the materials and fees, read off the final itself — and the deposit is the 50% this job asks for. With a refund due the button names both halves. Measured on a walkaway that paid its deposit and midpoint and had about thirty per cent of its hours logged: $9,187.50 received on a $12,250 Home Cleanout, $6,125 earned (the deposit, being more than the $3,600 of work), $3,062.50 due back, and the button reads Close — Retain $6,125 · refund $3,062.50 (re-measured 2026-10-02, P18, on an estimate in whole hours; on P17’s quarter hours the same walkaway read $9,009.38 received on $12,012.50, $6,006.25 earned and $3,003.13 due back).

The refund is recorded on the client’s card once it has been sent (since 2026-10-01). On a Closed — Deposit Retained hourly job the Timeline & Payments card carries a Walkaway settlement above the payments list: Received, Earned with the deposit and the work done it was worked from, Refunded once any is recorded, and Refund due, with Record refund while anything is due. The dialog opens on the amount due and today, and asks how it went (Cheque, Wire transfer or Bank transfer (ACH)), the reference and who it was paid to; it records who recorded it the way a payment is attributed. The refund is its own record on the payments list, beside the payments it does not change, and it is never deleted: one recorded in error is voided with a reason. More than is due asks first; more than the job holds is refused. Once it is recorded, the amount the job records as retained — on the rail’s one row, on the Win / Loss Won list and in its revenue — is what was received less what was refunded, and the refund due reads $0. Until it is recorded the retained amount is everything received, and the card names what is due back beside it. If the job’s estimate, or since 2026-10-06 (P22) its hours log, has not loaded on the device, the dialog and the card say so rather than print a figure — the work done is the hours logged, and a log not yet read is not zero hours. Nothing here applies to a fixed price.

The dialog also carries Delete entry permanently (test / mistake) — manager PIN, which removes the client and every record of the job from the app and the spreadsheet (the Drive folder is left alone) — for test or mistaken entries, never a lost client.

⚠⚠ A PART-PAID DEPOSIT IS MONEY RECEIVED (2026-09-29, workflow audit M4; Anthony, Q2: “keep it and name the amount”). The rule used to ask whether the deposit had been paid in full. So a client who had sent $2,000 of a $4,575 deposit and walked away was recorded as a plain loss, won was cleared, and the $2,000 appeared nowhere, Win / Loss included. It asks whether anything was received now, and names it.

⚠ Amending the reason does not move the date. Reopening the dialog on a job already closed out prefills the reason so it can be corrected; saving the correction used to restamp the loss to that day. The date the loss was recorded is written once.
⚠ THE ✕ IS WITHDRAWN ONCE THE JOB IS FINISHED (new 2026-09-22). Anthony: “after we won the client and have completed the job, the ‘x’ at the end of the client record in client dashboard should be deactivated … it is rightly there during the course of the job b/c the client could cancel mid-job, but once the final invoice is paid, it should not be there.” Two things withdraw it and either one alone is enough: the work has been delivered (the handover stamp set when a job is closed — a Re-open clears it, and the ✕ comes back with it), or a final payment is recorded. Nothing else about the row moves — the client is still listed and Open → is still there.

⚠ Why either one rather than both. A delivered job whose final invoice is outstanding is not a job we lost — the work was done, so that is a collections matter. Requiring both would have left exactly that case exposed, and it is the one where the wrong record is most tempting and most false.

⚠ What it was costing, measured on a delivered job that had paid in full. One press flipped a $19,940 engagement to Closed — Deposit Retained, wrote Price / estimate too high onto it as a loss reason, and collapsed its sixteen-row timeline to a single line reading “Closed — deposit retained · Price / estimate too high · $9,970 retained” — asserting we kept the deposit and walked away from work we had delivered and been paid for in full. On a job with no deposit recorded it is worse: the status becomes Lost, won is cleared, and every filter on the client list excludes a lost job, so the client drops off the list entirely and out of the won count.

⚠ Hidden rather than greyed out, and the rule sits on the handler as well as the button — so it cannot be reached around from the timeline or from any control added later. A job already marked Lost or Closed — Deposit Retained keeps it, because that is the route to amending the reason; a lost job lives under the Lost filter rather than in the default list.
What the agreements say about the money this dialog keeps. Both agreement forms make the deposit earned on signature and not refundable, on either billing basis, unless Havellin is in uncured material breach — so the retained deposit has its clause. Until 2026-09-30 (P16) that held on a fixed price only: on an hourly engagement the estate form made the deposit non-refundable only once the project had started, and the living-client form applied it to the amounts due and refunded any unused balance. Both hourly arms now say what the fixed arm says (§8; Anthony: “deposit paid, is lost”). Money above the deposit and the work, on an hourly job, goes back (since 2026-10-01). The hourly arm earns the hours worked and the materials used, with the deposit applied against them; until this the dialog kept every payment, so a midpoint above the work done by the day the client walked away was kept by the app and called earned by neither form. It is a refund due now, shown and recorded as above (Anthony: “the agreements already say so”). The fixed arm’s stage earn-out is unchanged. Every one of these clauses is drafted, not reviewed, until counsel signs off.

The 📁 Drive control on the Job Timeline heading has three states, and which one you get says what is true of the folder right now. The folder and all its subfolders are created once, automatically, when the client is created — on the Client Intake save, not at approval and not at activation.

What you seeWhat it meansCan you press it?
📁 DriveThe folder exists. Opens it.Yes — a real link, so middle-click, ⌘-click and copy-link all work.
📁 Creating Drive folder…It is being made right now. Google normally answers within a few seconds.No, deliberately. There is nothing to do but wait.
📁 Create Drive folderThere is no folder and none is being made — the automatic attempt at intake failed, and the badge at the time said why.Yes. This is the repair door, and the only thing in the app that makes a folder after intake.
⚠⚠ THE MIDDLE STATE IS NEW (2026-09-22), AND WITHOUT IT THE BUTTON RACED THE THING IT OFFERED TO DO. Anthony, on the first dummy client of the test run: “the old, create google drive folder button is stil there. but the folder is created automatically. when i clicked it, it changed to a drive link.” He was right that it should not have been there. The intake save fired the create and moved you to the Clients tab 800ms later (since 2026-09-23 it lands you on the new client’s own dashboard at once), while Apps Script cold-starts in seconds — so the whole gap between “client created” and “folder landed” showed a control asserting the folder did not exist, on a job where it was being made at that moment. ⚠ And pressing it sent a SECOND createFolder for the same job — measured in a browser at two calls for one client. Harmless on the current backend, which reuses a folder of the same name; that reuse exists because an older one did not, and one client ended up with four folders. Nothing done in that window caused any harm and there is nothing to clean up.
⚠ Two things this paragraph used to say had been false since 2026-09-11. It said the control is “only ever a link… there is never anything here to press to make one”, and that a folderless job “falls back to opening the Drive root”. The repair door was added that day and the root fallback was removed in the same change — it opened a folder holding none of the client’s files, which looks exactly like a working button, and is why a folderless client once got as far as a crew photographing the entryway before anybody noticed.

Change Orders

Until the signing packet goes out, a change in scope is an edit to the estimate (✎ Edit estimate, §9a), which goes back to the manager — and, if the price rises, back to the client. Once the packet has gone out the price is locked and a Change Order records the change. Open the client’s dashboard and press + New on the Change Orders card → description, a reason, and the additional Transition Concierge and Property Specialist hours (in whole hours, either role, added or taken off: the boxes step by 1, and since 2026-10-02 (P18; Anthony: “change orders whole hours”) Create Change Order refuses a part hour, naming it and saving nothing: “Change orders are in whole hours (1, 2, 3 …): concierge 2.5 is not a whole hour. Nothing was saved.” For P17’s one day they took quarter hours, and a change order recorded then keeps them) — on Home Prep, concierge hours, a preparation vendor, or both, and on a labour job whose estimate bundles prep the hours, a preparation vendor, or both (since 2026-09-30, P16; below) → Create Change Order. No manager PIN is involved — a Change Order is agreed with the client, not approved internally.

The accepted copy goes to Drive (2026-10-01; Anthony). When the client accepts, the change order — the printed page, with their typed name and the date — is filed into the client’s Change Orders folder as <HVL-ID> - Havellin Change Order CO-nnnnnn, with no date, so filing it again replaces it. The sync badge says it landed, and the row on the card then offers 📁 Filed copy, which opens it. If the filing does not land, or the change order was accepted before this date, the row offers 📁 File to Drive instead. Never before acceptance: an unaccepted change order is a proposal, and the folder holds what the client agreed to. Until this date nothing was filed there (§15).
⚠ A CHANGE ORDER CARRIES HOURS, NOT A PRICE (rebuilt 2026-09-11). Anthony: “the change order is supposed to trigger when we’re gonna run fifteen percent over a job, and that’s on the transition concierge and property specialist hours … I don’t think the change order should have a dollar amount, and we certainly shouldn’t bill a client for it on the change order. It should be an estimate of the additional hours … added to the final invoice once those hours are actually billed.” There is no dollar field on the form for the work — you type hours, on either billing basis (the one money box, since 2026-09-30, is an added preparation vendor’s estimated cost, below: on Home Prep, and since P16 on a labour job whose estimate bundles prep). On a time-and-materials job the printed copy carries no $ anywhere — it states the hours on the approved estimate, the change, and the revised hours, and says outright that it does not itself create a charge. (On a fixed price it carries its price, since 2026-09-25 — see the next note.)

On a time-and-materials job — which is every service by default — the change order is billed by the TIMESHEET. The crew works the added scope, logs the hours, and the final invoice trues labour to the log. The change is already in the bill; the change order is the client’s written authority for it, not a second charge. On a fixed-price job it is the other way round: a flat fee never consults the timesheet, so there the hours are converted at the estimate’s rates — plus the 20% expedited-delivery premium on an expedited job, since 2026-09-30 (the last note) — and added to the fixed fee. The invoice says which: “billed in the hours above” or “added to the fixed project fee above”. Since 2026-09-25 the fixed-price change order carries that figure on its face, where the client signs it.

⚠ On a time-and-materials job the 15% is why the screen exists, so it says so as you type. The modal reads “140.0 hrs on the estimate becomes 180.0 (+28.6%) — Past the 15% threshold” in amber, or “+2.9% — inside the 15% the client already agreed to” in blue. On a fixed price the same readout gives the price instead (next note). A change order with no hours on it is refused — it would put a row on the timeline and the invoice reading as an agreed change while agreeing to nothing. (On Home Prep since 2026-09-30, and on a labour job whose estimate bundles prep since P16 the same day, one that adds a preparation vendor instead is saved: it agrees to that vendor.)

⚠ Accepting one moves the baseline, so the ±15% variance gate stops firing on scope the client signed for — but hours beyond the authorised scope still trip it, and an unaccepted change order moves nothing. Unsigned scope cannot excuse an overrun. Since 2026-09-25 that holds on every hours readout in the app, not only the invoice (the note after next).

Anything raised before 2026-09-11 carried a dollar amount and those fields are gone. Prelaunch, so there is nothing to migrate; if you are looking at a printed copy from before that date, throw it away and print it again.
⚠⚠ ON A FIXED PRICE THE TRIGGER IS SCOPE, NEVER THE 15% — AND THE CHANGE ORDER CARRIES ITS PRICE (2026-09-25). Anthony: “Is a 15% over run change order necessary on a fixed price job, or should it be a ‘Change of Scope’ or something that triggers a change order?” Scope. The agreements already said so — the estate form’s §4.1 fixed arm lists work beyond the Exhibit A scope in place of the hours trigger, the standard form’s §3.8 fixed arm is headed Changes to Scope, and the fixed-price estimate’s Terms promise “any price adjustment agreed in writing before the additional work proceeds” — but seven screens still spoke the time-and-materials rule on a flat-fee job:
WhereWhat it said on a fixed-price jobWhat it says now
The change-order screen, as you type“+2.9% — inside the 15% the client already agreed to, so this records the scope rather than changing what they owe”, and the invoice then added it to the feeThe price: “+20.0 concierge / +20.0 specialist hrs at $150 / $100 an hour — + $5,000 on the fixed project fee, which goes from $24,000 to $29,000”. On an expedited job, since 2026-09-30: “… an hour plus the 20% expedited-delivery premium, as this job is expedited — + $6,000 on the fixed project fee, which goes from $24,000 to $30,000” (the last note)
The note above the hours boxes“A change order carries no price and bills nothing on its own”“On a fixed price the change order is the charge … The trigger is a change in scope, never the hours.” On an expedited job it says the hours are priced “at the job’s rates plus the 20% expedited-delivery premium, because this job is expedited” (since 2026-09-30)
Get Acceptance, where the client types their name“No charge is created by this change order”The change to the fixed fee, the revised fee, and the hours and rates it was priced at — with the premium, on an expedited job (since 2026-09-30)
The printed change orderHours only, and “This change order does not itself create a charge”The fee in the agreement, change orders already accepted, this one’s price with its hours and rates (and the premium, on an expedited job since 2026-09-30), and the revised fee
The Hours Log on the Client Dashboard, past 15%“Client must be notified in writing before continuing work — per agreement terms”Ours to absorb, out of the margin; a change order only for work beyond the agreed scope
The hours summary on the Job Plan, past 15%“Client must be notified before continuing work”The same sentence as the dashboard — one sentence, read by both
The projection card, and the line under the hours fold“⛔ STOP — Change Order Required … No further scope work until a signed Change Order”“⚠ Over the estimated hours — margin at risk”: a margin warning, never a stop
Why the price is printed on a fixed-price change order, when on 2026-09-11 it came off. That rule was right for the reason it gave: on time and materials the timesheet already bills the hours, so a price on the change order charged the same work twice. On a fixed price nothing else charges them — the final invoice adds the change order’s price, and it is the only thing that does. And the living-client agreement’s fixed arm states no hourly rate anywhere, so a flat-fee client was signing a change order in hours and then being billed those hours at rates they had never seen in writing. The price on the page is what the client signs, what the invoice adds, and what that agreement’s §3.8 already promised: “any resulting price adjustment will be documented in a written Change Order.” A time-and-materials change order still carries no price.

Each change order is priced once, and the invoice adds those prices up. At Premium Estate rates half an hour is $92.50, which is $93 on each of two change orders; the invoice used to round the total instead ($185), a dollar off what two signed pages said. The fixed-price invoice now prints each accepted change order’s own figure in an Amount column and adds them. On an expedited job the premium goes on before that one rounding (since 2026-09-30), so the figure on the page is still exactly what the invoice adds.

Found on the way: the printed change order and the acceptance screen printed the reason’s internal key, so a client signed a page reading Reason: scope_add. They print Scope addition — new rooms or services now, on both billing bases. If you printed a fixed-price change order before 2026-09-25, print it again — the old one says it creates no charge, and the invoice charges it.
⚠⚠ AN ACCEPTED CHANGE ORDER IS PART OF THE HOURS THE JOB IS MEASURED AGAINST, ON EVERY SCREEN (2026-09-25). Until this date only the final invoice moved with a change order. The Hours Log bars on the Client Dashboard, the hours summary on the Job Plan, the Hours fold, the desk card’s hours line, the projection card and the schedule strip all measured against the estimate alone — so a time-and-materials job holding a signed +40-hour change order read OVERAGE and ⛔ STOP — Change Order Required over the very hours the client had just signed for, and a fixed-price job read margin at risk on hours the change order had priced. The schedule strip’s own advice — raise a change order if the scope grew — could never clear the flag it was attached to, because accepting one moved nothing.
What an accepted change order does now
The hours logged are measured againstThe estimate plus every accepted change order, on all six readouts. Each says so where the figure is printed: incl. +40.0 hrs by change order under a dashboard bar; Estimated hours include +20.0 concierge / +20.0 specialist hrs from 1 accepted change order under the Job Plan’s hours table and on the projection card.
The plan’s lengthLengthened by the working days the change order’s hours take, and the strip says incl. 2 days by change order beside the count. A quoted length stays the base; only the change order’s share is worked out, and the plan is never shorter than one day.
Work doneUnchanged, deliberately. It is still the room statuses against the estimate’s rooms — nothing marks a change order’s hours complete, so folding them in would leave a finished job reading short of 100% forever.
The estimate and the agreementUnchanged. They state what was quoted and signed; the change order is the record of what moved.
An unaccepted change orderMoves nothing, anywhere. A draft authorises nothing, so it must not raise the line the crew is measured against.
⚠ A second change order is measured from where the first left the job. Its readout says “180.0 hrs on the estimate and 1 accepted change order becomes 200.0 (+11.1%)”, and the printed time-and-materials change order carries a Change orders already accepted row between the estimate and this change, so its revised total is the real one. It used to print a revised figure short by every hour the earlier change orders had added, on the page the client signs next.

On a fee-only Home Prep job the readout no longer says there is no estimate. Corrected the same day: this paragraph went on to say the screen tells you hours on a prep change order are billed nowhere, and not to promise a prep client extra concierge hours once they have signed, because nothing could add them. Both went false with the note below — a change order is the route now, and it bills.
⚠⚠ ON HOME PREP A CHANGE ORDER ADDS CONCIERGE HOURS AT A STATED RATE, AND THE FINAL BILLS THEM (2026-09-25). Anthony, on a prep job signed with vendors only: “if we have a live job that only quoted vendors, there’s no way to then add transition concierge hours” — correct until this date. The estimate locks at signature and a prep change order billed nothing, so the half-day of clearing a room before the painters arrive had no route to the invoice. It does now:
On a Home Prep job
The formConcierge hours, never specialist hours. The specialist box is greyed out (Not used on Home Prep) — the engagement has no crew — and Save refuses specialist hours and one that would take the job’s concierge hours below zero. Until 2026-09-30 it also refused a change order with no concierge hours; one may now add a preparation vendor instead, or as well (next note).
The readout as you type“+6.0 concierge hrs at $150 an hour — about $900 at the estimated hours, billed as they are worked on the final invoice, on top of the 30% Home Sale Preparation Fee on the prep vendors’ invoices.” On a job that quoted no concierge hours it adds that the rate is the one the agreement states in §3.3, and that the change order prints it again.
The page the client signsConcierge hours on the approved estimate, any change orders already accepted, this change, the revised concierge hours, and Rate for these hours — $150 an hour, billed as worked. It restates the rate the agreement’s §3.3 names — until that clause began stating it (2026-09-25, §8) this page was the only place a vendors-only client saw one. The acceptance panel names the rate as well.
Once acceptedThe Job Plan opens its hours log, concierge row only, and Budget & Fee shows the hours added (§6c). Confirm the team in the log, then log the hours as they are worked.
The final invoiceThe 30% fee on the vendors’ actual invoices plus the logged concierge hours at the rate, with a line under the change orders saying so. Coordinating the vendors stays inside the fee. Nothing logged, and it bills the fee alone — it warns rather than refuses (§12).
The estimate and the agreementUnchanged — they state what was quoted and signed. The agreement’s §3.3 names this route, and the concierge rate it bills at, in writing from today (§8).
⚠ A draft moves nothing: until the client accepts, no log opens and nothing is billed. The rate is the estimate’s own concierge rate — $185 on a Premium Estate job. A change in what a vendor already on the job charges still needs no change order: the fee follows what the vendors invoice. A vendor the estimate did not carry is added by change order (next note, since 2026-09-30).
⚠⚠ A HOME PREP CHANGE ORDER CAN ADD A PREPARATION VENDOR (2026-09-30). Anthony, asked whether a prep change order may add a new vendor: “yes, add new also, or add TC hours for decluttering.” Until this date a painter found mid-job had no way onto a signed prep engagement: the Job Plan’s sourcing list, its budget card and the final invoice’s Home Sale Preparation Fee all read the approved estimate’s prep lines and nothing else. On Home Prep for Sale the change-order form now carries Add a prep vendor (the Vendor Directory’s Property Preparation categories) and Its estimated cost, and a change order carries concierge declutter hours, a vendor, or both:
A change order that adds a vendor
The formPick the vendor’s category and type its estimated cost. A vendor needs its cost — “Enter the added vendor’s estimated cost, so the client sees the fee it carries” — and a change order with neither hours nor a vendor is refused: “Enter the additional concierge hours this change is expected to take, or the prep vendor it adds — or both.” With the Vendor Directory not loaded the list reads Vendor Directory not loaded and offers nothing to pick.
The readout as you type“Adds Painting to the preparation vendors. It bills the client directly at cost, and the 30% Home Sale Preparation Fee applies to what it actually charges — about $1,350 at the estimated $4,500. Once the client accepts, it joins the prep vendors on the Job Plan to be booked and quoted.”
Get AcceptanceAdded vendor — Painting, est. $4,500, billed to you directly and 30% Home Sale Preparation Fee on it — about $1,350, on its actual invoice; the sentence the client types their name under names the vendor and the fee.
The page the client signsThe vendor, its estimated cost and the Home Sale Preparation Fee at that cost, beside the concierge hours and their rate when it carries any. It drops “vendor costs are unaffected”, which would be false on it, and says the other preparation vendors’ costs are unaffected instead.
The Change Orders cardadds Painting (est. $4,500), after the hours when it carries any.
Once the client acceptsThe vendor joins the job’s prep lines: the Job Plan’s sourcing list (book it and type its Quote $), the Budget & Fee card (§6c) and the close-out checklist’s Prep vendors confirmed count.
The final invoiceThe 30% fee on what that vendor actually charged — its Quote $ on the Job Plan, its estimated cost until one is typed — inside the one Home Sale Preparation Fee row with the other prep vendors. Its change-order row notes “Adds Painting (est. $4,500) — it bills you directly, and the Home Sale Preparation Fee on it is in the fee above”, the payment summary’s change-order line reads adds Painting, in the Home Sale Preparation Fee above, and the ±15% check counts the fee at its estimated cost as authorised (§12).
⚠ Until the client accepts, the vendor is nowhere — not on the sourcing list, not in the budget, not on the invoice. It was standalone Home Prep for Sale only until P16, the same day: a labour job whose estimate bundles prep may add one too (next note). Concierge declutter hours on a prep change order work exactly as the note above describes.
⚠⚠ AND ON A LABOUR JOB WHOSE ESTIMATE BUNDLES PREP (2026-09-30, P16). Anthony, asked whether a job carrying prep vendors on a labour service — a Home Transition, a Cleanout, an estate — may add one by change order as Home Prep does: “yes”. The form shows Add a prep vendor and Its estimated cost under the hours wherever the estimate carries a prep vendor with a cost — which is where the agreement prints its prep fee clause — on an hourly quote, or on a fixed price whose prep fee is a line on top of the fee (an estimate saved from 2026-09-23). Not on an older fixed fee that carries the prep fee inside it, where a vendor added later could have no fee inside a figure set before it, and never on a job whose estimate has no costed prep vendor: its agreement says Havellin adds no fee or markup to vendor invoices. One rule decides it (coPrepVendorsOn), and the save asks it again. A change order carries hours, a vendor, or both:
On a labour job with prep bundled
The formThe note above the hours adds “A change order can also add a preparation vendor found mid-job, with or without hours: it bills the client directly at cost, and the 30% Home Sale Preparation Fee applies to what it actually charges. It books no concierge hours.” (“… on top of the fixed project fee” on a fixed price). A vendor needs its estimated cost, and one with neither hours nor a vendor is refused: “Enter the additional hours this change is expected to take — concierge, specialist or both, or the preparation vendor it adds. Negative to reduce scope.”
The readout as you typeThe hours readout as before — the ±15% line on an hourly job, the price on a fixed one — with the vendor’s own line under it, “Adds Landscaper to the preparation vendors. It bills the client directly at cost, and the 30% Home Sale Preparation Fee applies to what it actually charges — about $2,700 at the estimated $9,000. Once the client accepts, it joins the prep vendors on the Job Plan to be booked and quoted.”, or that line alone when it carries no hours.
Get AcceptanceThe vendor’s two rows, as on Home Prep, and the sentence the client types their name under adds “… and the addition of Landscaper to the preparation vendors, billed to them directly at cost, with the 30% Home Sale Preparation Fee on what it actually charges”. On a fixed price it adds that the vendor “is not part of the fixed project fee”. A change order that adds only a vendor shows no fee change, its hours row reads no hours change, and neither sentence speaks of hours.
The page the client signsOne table — the hours (hourly) or the fixed-fee rows (fixed price) when it carries any, then the vendor and the fee at its estimated cost — and terms ending “That fee covers coordinating and supervising the preparation work, which is not billed as hours. The other vendors’ costs are unaffected by this change.” (“That fee is charged in addition to your fixed project fee and covers …” on a fixed price). A vendor-only change order prints no rush or discount line: that line is about hours.
Once the client acceptsThe vendor joins the job’s prep lines: the Home Prep for Sale lines on the Job Plan’s sourcing list (book it, type its Quote $) and the vendors lined up count. It books no concierge hours, so it moves neither the hours budget nor the plan’s length.
The invoicesFrom the midpoint on, the 30% on its Quote $ (its estimated cost until one is typed) inside the one prep fee row, on either basis; on a fixed price that row sits on top of the fee. The final’s change-order section names it, and the ±15% baseline moves by that fee at the estimated cost, so an accepted vendor never sends the final to a manager (§12).
Both agreement forms say so before the client signs (§8): the fee is charged on the preparation vendors “identified in the Estimate or added by Change Order” (“in Exhibit A” on the estate form). Measured on an hourly Home Transition with a $10,000 painter on its estimate: an accepted $9,000 landscaper adds $2,700 to what the job collects and reads no variance; with its quote typed at $12,000, $3,600.

The change order then appears as its own row on that card, with its buttons on the row:

  1. PDF — the printable Change Order, on every row, before the client agrees and after. On a time-and-materials job it shows the hours on the approved estimate, the scope change and the revised estimated hours, and no dollar figure appears on it. On a fixed price it shows the fixed fee in the agreement, any change orders already accepted, this one’s price with the hours and rates it was priced at (and the expedited-delivery premium on an expedited job, since 2026-09-30), and the revised fixed fee. On Home Prep it is the page the two notes above describe: the concierge hours and their rate, and any vendor it adds with its estimated cost and the fee at that cost.
  2. Get Acceptance — the client types their name against ✓ I Accept This Change Order. Hand them the iPad, or record it yourself off their email or call. Name and date are stored on the change order. On a fixed price the panel they sign under states the change to the fixed fee and the revised fee; on Home Prep it names the rate for the hours and any vendor added, with its estimated cost and the fee on it. It is offered only while the change order is waiting on the client; once accepted the row reads Accepted, names who accepted it, and keeps its PDF.
An unaccepted Change Order is never billed. Acceptance is what updates the project total and what puts the change on the final invoice — the invoice counts client-approved change orders and silently ignores the rest. A change order created, printed, agreed on the phone and never marked accepted is work you will do and not charge for. Take the acceptance at the moment the client agrees.
⚠⚠ UNTIL 2026-09-29 NEITHER BUTTON COULD BE PRESSED, SO EVERY CHANGE ORDER RAISED BEFORE THEN IS STILL UNACCEPTED. The 2026-09-28 workflow audit found that PDF and Get Acceptance lived only in an expandable row on the client list that the app built and never put on the page. The Change Orders card read Awaiting acceptance with nothing to press. So a change order could be created and never printed or accepted — and an unaccepted change order moves nothing: the client had no page to sign, its hours never lengthened the plan or cleared the overrun flags, a fixed-price change never reached the final invoice, and a vendors-only Home Prep job never opened its hours log. Open each job that has one, press PDF on its row, walk the client through it, and take Get Acceptance — or record it off their email or call if they have already agreed. The hidden row is deleted, along with the one function that only it read.

The notices moved with the buttons. After Create Change Order and after I Accept This Change Order, the notice prints on the dashboard above the timeline and the card redraws at once. Both used to print on the Build Estimate screen, which is not showing when you are on the dashboard, so pressing Create looked as though nothing had happened and the card went on reading None issued — and the Create notice told you to open the Client Dashboard you were already on. If you raised a change order twice because of that, print and accept only one — a change order cannot be deleted, and the one left unaccepted moves nothing and is never billed.
⚠ WHAT THE PRINTED CHANGE ORDER SAYS ABOUT THE RUSH PREMIUM AND THE DISCOUNT (2026-09-29; the fixed-price premium changed 2026-09-30). One line, and only on a job that carries either. On a time-and-materials job the change order’s hours are billed on the final invoice like every other hour, so they carry the job’s expedited-delivery premium and its preferred-client discount: “Like every other hour on this engagement, these hours carry the 20% expedited-delivery premium and the 10% preferred-client discount on the final invoice.” On a fixed price the preferred-client discount never applies to a change order, and since 2026-09-30 an expedited job’s change order carries the premium (Anthony, asked whether a fixed-price rush job’s change-order hours carry the 20%: “yes”). A new change order on an expedited fixed price whose premium is a line on the fee — an estimate saved from 2026-09-30 — is priced at its hours at the plain rates plus 20%, rounded once: $2,000 of hours prints as $2,400, and the final invoice adds exactly that. Its line reads “It is priced at the hourly rates shown plus the 20% expedited-delivery premium, as this engagement is expedited; the preferred-client discount on your fixed project fee does not apply to it.”, ending at expedited on a job with no discount. The premium is pinned on the change order when it is created, so a later edit to the estimate never reprices one already raised, and a change order raised before 2026-09-30, or on an older fixed fee that carries its premium inside it, keeps its plain-rate price and the line it was printed with: “It is priced at the plain hourly rates shown: the expedited-delivery premium and the preferred-client discount in your fixed project fee do not apply to it.” — reading on your fixed project fee where they are lines on it. A discount offered after approval with Offer Discount counts: on an older fee it is folded into the fee, on a newer one it is a line on it. On Home Prep, which never carries the rush premium, the line names the discount alone. The percentages are the ones the estimate was priced at, not today’s setting. A job with neither prints no line, and the acceptance panel does not repeat it (on an expedited fixed price its own Priced at … line names the premium). Both agreement forms say the same on an expedited fixed price (§8): the rush clause ends “… as itemized on the Estimate, and on the price of any Change Order.”, and the estate form’s blank change-order form prices a change at “(the additional hours at the rates in Section 3.1, plus the twenty percent (20%) expedited-delivery premium)”. Hourly jobs are unchanged: a change order there carries hours and no price.

9c. 🔍 Walkthrough — reading the estimate back

The middle control on the Job Timeline & Payments heading line, between ✎ Edit Client and 📁 Drive. It opens the walkthrough that priced the job as a read-only page over the dashboard: the private walkthrough notes, every room that was ticked with its fullness and complexity scores and its note, the notable collections with their dispositions, the vehicles, and any prep scope notes. The intended use is the one Ashley described — reading yourself back into a job the night before Day 1, or in the car outside.

On the pageWhere it came from
Private walkthrough notes, first, above everythingThe internal box at the end of the room grid (§5a)
Rooms, in walkthrough order, grouped by sectionThe room grid — scores, hours and the per-room note
A room reading Out of scopeTicked ✗ — the client is handling it. It is an answer, not a gap, so no score prints against it
Notable collections and their dispositionsThe collections card
Vehicles, with collector and title to be locatedThe vehicles card
Prep scope notesThe Property Preparation lines — on a fee-only job these are the walkthrough
⚠⚠ IT IS AN INTERNAL PAGE AND IT CANNOT BE SENT, FILED OR PRINTED. It carries the private note word for word — what a family is like, who is hoarding, which son disagrees with which daughter. So unlike the five client documents in §9b it is not in the document registry at all: there is no Send, no File to Drive, and the viewer’s Print / Save PDF button is hidden on it. A printed copy of that left on a kitchen counter is the failure this shape exists to make impossible. The page stamps itself Internal walkthrough record — not a client document in red across the top, the same way the Drive worksheet does.
⚠ ON AN APPROVED ESTIMATE IT SHOWS THE FROZEN COPY, AND SAYS SO. Approval takes an immutable snapshot of every room — scores, hours and notes — so later edits cannot rewrite history. The page shows that snapshot and prints Locked <date> by <name>. An estimate that has not been approved shows the live rooms under an amber line saying the scores can still change. Standing in a driveway you need to know which of the two you are reading.
The button is withheld on a job with no estimate — the fix there is to go and build one, and a button that only refuses is worse than none. ⚠ But it is offered while the estimates are still loading, and the message then says so rather than claiming the job has no walkthrough. On a device with a cold cache — a new iPad, cleared data, Safari evicting storage — a missing button would be a false statement about the job when the truth is about the device.

10. Active Job Documentation

On the Job Plan tab once a job is Won. The rooms are the In the house stage: second on the thread after Before Day 1, below the gate chips and the two folded tools (§11). Home Prep for Sale has no rooms and no camera: its plan is the vendor and budget view (§6c), and its Job Admin & Inv tab carries no inventory (§10a).

Corrected 2026-08-03 — this said Active, and the app used to enforce it. Photo capture now opens as soon as the job is Won, matching the gate the Job Plan screen itself enforces. It mattered: a job sits at Won for the entire stretch between the client accepting and the deposit being recorded, the Job Plan renders its room cards — camera buttons included — that whole time, and every shot taken in that window was silently discarded. No upload, no record, no message: the camera opened, the photo was taken, and the app did nothing. Day-one walkthrough photos evaporated. If either gate ever moves, move both.
Three refusals that were silent now speak. A job that isn't Won, a job that can't be resolved, and a failed photo whose image data is no longer held all now say so. A camera button that does nothing is indistinguishable from one that worked, which is why these were worth more than a log line.
⚠⚠ A JOB PLAN STAGE THAT SHUT ITSELF WITHIN SECONDS (fixed 2026-09-11). Reported from a job: “when i expand a job plan stage, it automatically shuts on me within seconds. i can’t do anything.” Two faults, either of which alone made the tab unusable. The tab was re-entering itself. After rendering, it pulls anything another device photographed and redraws if that changed something — but “changed” was true the moment the sheet held any photo for the job, so on a job with photos on file it re-rendered forever, one network round trip per cycle. It was harmless until there were photographs in the sheet to come back. Measured on the real page: 21 re-renders and 20 round trips before, one of each after. And any redraw shut every open phase, because the accordion lived only in the markup — and the Job Plan legitimately redraws for several reasons (a photo landing from the other device, the estimate store arriving, a directory going stale). Which folds you have open is remembered for the session, so those redraws are invisible. Since 2026-09-20 only the two tools at the top fold (Vendors & partners, Hours & daily close); the stages of the job are always open (§11).
⚠ A photo can no longer be stuck on Uploading… (2026-09-11). A browser request has no time limit, so a Drive upload that never came back left the room card reading Uploading… for the rest of the job — no count, no error, no Retry, nothing to press. There is a 90-second watchdog on it now: a request that has not answered by then is recorded as failed, says so, and points at Retry — the image is still held on the device, so it goes up as soon as the cause is fixed.
⚠ Any shot but an as-found one can be deleted, from the job and from Drive (2026-09-20). Anthony, in a room: “i took a crummy picture and i want to be able to delete it.” Until this date the only photograph you could remove was one that had failed to reach Drive, with a ✕ beside Retry. Now every thumbnail in the room workspace carries a 🗑 in its corner, and so does the last shot in the camera. It asks first, and says what goes with the shot: its detail shots, and, by name, any line split out of the photograph. The shot comes off the job straight away. If it reached Drive, the file moves to Google Drive’s bin, which keeps it for 30 days; if Drive refuses, a red message says the file is off the job but still in the client’s folder. Removed items on the Job Admin & Inv tab gives the lines back, but not the image (§10a). As-found shots cannot be deleted from either bin: they are the record of the room before anything was touched, and a poor frame does no harm there, so the bin refuses and says to shoot another.
A failed photo now survives the session. Its Retry used to do nothing at all once the tab had been closed — the image was held in memory only, so a shot that failed on Tuesday was gone by Wednesday with a live-looking Retry button sitting beside it. Failed shots now keep their image data on the device (under their own storage key, so image data can never crowd out the inventory record) until they upload. If the device has no room to hold one, you are told to retry it now, before leaving the job.
Photo counts are correct on the first open of a job. The room cards used to be drawn before the photo records were loaded, so the first time you opened any job in a session every count read zero and every failed-upload flag was hidden — photos correctly filed in Drive read on screen as photos that never happened. Related: uploads now resolve the destination through the shared folder resolver, so a job folder created before the 2026-07-15 Photos + Asset Documentation merge files correctly instead of failing every photo (§15).

The room list and the room workspace (rebuilt 2026-09-19)

The rooms are a block of cards, three across on a desk (two on the iPad, one on a phone), the In the house card on the plan — second on the thread, after Before Day 1, open at all times — one card per in-scope room in walkthrough order, carrying the name and the status pill on the top line, the shot counts (2 found · 5 items · 1 after) under it, and the walkthrough note from the estimate on one truncated line. Rooms marked out of scope on the estimate are named in a single Not in scope — homeowner is handling line and are not rows. Tapping a card opens the room workspace as a pop-up over the plan — a dialog no wider than 760px on a desk, dimmed plan behind it, closed by the ← Rooms button, a tap outside it or Esc; on a phone it is the whole screen — with three camera buttons (As found · Items · After), the As-found pass complete tick under them, the Lock and Cleared taps, and a strip of the room’s shots grouped by pass, each with a 🗑 and, on a failed one, Retry. A detail shot is counted on its item’s tile (+1 detail) and has a tile of its own only if it failed to reach Drive, under Detail shots not saved, naming the item it is a close-up of — since 2026-09-30; until then a failed detail shot was drawn nowhere, so its Retry and 🗑 were out of reach once the camera moved on, while the room card read ⚠ not saved for good. The old per-room cards — two grids, five statuses, a typed name and category before every shot — are gone.

Why the rooms are never inside a fold — and no longer at the top either. Every stage started closed, and the first cut put the room list inside Phase 1 — so the whole field surface sat behind a tap on a bar reading Phase 1, which the browser check found when it could not click a room at all. That morning’s fix put the rooms above every fold, and Anthony read it, correctly, as out of order: the vendor calls and the pre-job call come before the rooms in a real job. The rooms now sit in their place in the order (§11), open at all times — and since 2026-09-20 so does every other stage of the job: only the two tools above it, Vendors & partners and Hours & daily close, fold. The rooms are the work; the stages are the checklist, and neither is behind a bar.
The intake brief is in the room now (new 2026-09-19). The Standing job flags brief — every must-find with its Found it tick, the safety answer, cash, safes, documents, the lot — is the first thing in the workspace’s scrolling body, above the pass instructions and the three cameras, so whoever is shooting the study reads “the coin collection is in the study safe” standing in the study rather than two taps back on the plan header. The red firearms line stays pinned above the body, where a scroll cannot hide it, so the brief in the room carries no second copy of it; a job whose only flag is firearms shows the pinned line and nothing else.

Three passes per room, and nothing typed in the field

PassWhat is shotWhat it becomes
As foundWide shots from the corners, then every drawer, cabinet, closet and box opened and photographed where it sits, undisturbed.Evidence of what the house held on Day 1. Filed as before in the client’s As-Found Record folder; never an inventory line, and never deleted. Mandatory on estate and probate jobs: Lock is refused until at least one exists and the As-found pass complete box under the cameras is ticked (the tick since 2026-09-20; lockRefusal, keyed on isDecedentJob). The app cannot count drawers, so a person attests the pass: the tick records the date and the job’s concierge, and can be unticked. On a living-client job either gap is an amber flag (lockFlag).
ItemsAfter sorting: one shot per object, shelf or obvious lot, with one chip lit — Keep · New home · To a person · Donate · Sell · Remove · Undecided — and, per shot, ⚑ Appraise, Detail of last and a spoken or typed note. New home is offered only where the job has one to go to (a Home Transition, or any job with a new address recorded); To a person is offered on every job, and on an estate it is the bequest release. (Five chips until 2026-09-21.)One inventory line per shot to start with (at the desk one photograph can carry several lines, §10a), with no object name and the default category. The chip maps onto the desk’s nine dispositions (New home → Move, To a person → Distribute, Remove → Junk, Undecided → blank). A detail shot files under the previous item (groupId) and is not a line. The note lands as fieldNote.
AfterThe empty room.Filed as after. Cleared flags without one.
  • The chip latches; Appraise and Detail reset. A disposition is a standing answer about the shelf being worked; send this one to a specialist and this is a close-up of the last one are judgements about a single object, so both clear after every shot rather than latching on and quietly flagging the rest of the room. Undecided is the default, so an item nobody decided in the house lands in Not yet decided at the top of the Job Admin & Inv tab, which is the honest place for it.
  • The camera stays open until Done. It is the app’s own viewfinder (getUserMedia), not the phone’s camera app — the native picker closes after every shot and cannot be kept open, which is the whole reason the in-app camera exists. Where camera access is refused the same overlay degrades to the native picker one shot at a time and says so; every shot still files.
  • Every shot uploads as it is taken. As-found shots file to the client’s As-Found Record folder (since 2026-09-20; a job folder made before then has none, so they go to Estate Inventory until somebody creates As-Found Record in it by hand in Drive, which the app then picks up). Item, detail and after shots file to Estate Inventory. The 90-second watchdog and Retry work as before, and any shot but an as-found one can be deleted (above). The manifest sync is scheduled for items and details.
  • Filename: HVL-2609-K7QX_Kitchen_INV_3_2026-09-19_184821.jpg — job, room, pass (before / INV / DETAIL / after), sequence within the room and pass, timestamp. It used to carry the typed object name and category; it carries neither now, because neither exists in the field.
  • The note on the last shot saves itself; there is no Add button (2026-09-20). A typed box is always there (Note on the last shot — saved automatically), with 🎤 Hold to talk beside it where the browser offers speech recognition. A spoken note is saved the moment you let go. A typed note is saved by Enter, by the next shutter or by Done, and it goes on the shot that was last when you started typing, even if you shoot the next one before you finish. Either way the words are added to that shot’s note, never written over it. Until this date the app’s own Done threw a typed note away (the phone keyboard’s done key saved it), and the Add button was one more step.

Room status: three states, two taps

A room is pending, locked (sorted and shot — the decisions are made) or cleared (the specialists have finished, it is empty and the after shot is taken). Cleared is offered only on a locked room. Both taps live in the room workspace beside the camera. The five-state ladder (pending / sorting / locked / packed / complete) and the two grids that enforced it are gone: a plan written on the old build reads sorting as pending and packed or complete as cleared, on read, without rewriting the stored record (roomStatusNormalize). Work done on the Client Dashboard earns a room’s concierge hours at locked and its specialist hours at cleared, exactly as it used to at packed.

Notable Collections — Asset Documentation

Each collection flagged on the estimate has a card under Collection Partners in the Job Plan’s Vendors & partners fold, with two buttons:

  • 📷 Capture Asset Photo — condition and identification photography, filed to Estate Inventory as HVL-YYMM-XXXX_ASSET_<Collection>_<n>_<YYYY-MM-DD>_<HHMMSS>.jpg.
  • 📄 Upload Appraisal / Offer — an appraisal, a dealer’s or buyer’s offer or a receipt, PDF or image, filed to Estate Inventory as HVL-YYMM-XXXX_APPRSL_<Collection>_<n>_<YYYY-MM-DD>_<HHMMSS>.<ext>.

⚑ Appraise — the second avenue

The toggle beside the chips is not another chip, and that is the whole design: an appraisal is not a destination, it is the step before one. Press it and the item goes to the Appraisal Worklist while keeping whatever disposition you chipped — first appraise, then auction or consign or sell. In the field the app knows nothing about the object (no name, no category), so the automatic category rule that routes art, jewelry, silver, antiques, rugs, collectibles, firearms, wine and instruments to the worklist fires at the desk, once the line has been named and categorised on the Job Admin & Inv tab, and only on an estate (§10a). The toggle is for what no category rule will ever see — the period side table, the unmarked bronze — and it is the only way to record that judgement while standing in front of the object. Missed in the house, Needs Appraisal in the item panel’s Valuation section does the same thing that evening. It resets after every shot and the chip deliberately does not: a disposition is a standing answer about the shelf, this is a judgement about one object.

10a. Job Admin & Inventory (Tab)

Tab: Job Admin & Inv → select a client. A per-client tangible-personal-property manifest, and — on a decedent job — the estate documentation workspace. The manifest itself runs on every labour service; only the fiduciary half is estate-only (see below). It assembles from three sources: the shots taken on the Items pass in the room workspace (they arrive unnamed — see below), + Add line item (an asset with no photo — cash, account, a vehicle), and import from the estimate (below).

The two intake questions sit above Job Admin (new 2026-09-19). The desk names that evening’s shots against the must-find list, so the list is there: From intake — must find · safety, each must-find with its Found it tick, then the safety answer. Only those two; cash, safes and the other ticked rows stay on the Job Plan. It renders nothing on a job with neither answer.
Job Admin — folded at the top of the tab (new 2026-09-19). The desk paperwork that used to be checkboxes on the Job Plan: Financial close (vendor invoices filed; on disposal jobs settlement timelines confirmed; and, until 2026-10-03, proceeds reconciled and donation receipts, which are lines the app reads since P19), the Florida court & legal compliance list (six items, from the §733.604 inventory verified to the final accounting — seven until 2026-10-03, when the PR’s sign-off box went, below — which of them render is decided by the matter type and the engagement tier, not by the service; see below), on a trust matter the trustee’s own Trust administration list (new 2026-10-03, below) and Records (the seven-year archive). Above the boxes are the lines the app reads for itself — first, since 2026-10-03, any original will or cash recorded on the Job Plan’s Found on site card (§11), in red while Havellin holds it; then rooms cleared, on an estate with property released to a person Signed receipt for every item released to a person (new 2026-10-03), hours logged, change orders accepted, final invoice sent, and since 2026-10-03 the Disposition Ledger signed on an estate, donation receipts line by line and sale proceeds reconciled (Close-out records, below) — and the fold carries the count (0 of 3 ticked · 3 derived items still open on a Home Cleanout with nothing done yet; it read 0 of 5 before two of its boxes became lines). It renders only on a won job and, deliberately, only here: it is inventory paperwork, and the Job Admin & Inv tab is where the concierge already is in the middle of a job. Opening it never creates a plan record.
Unnamed shots is the first worklist count (new 2026-09-19). Every Items-pass shot arrives with no object name, so its row opens with an empty name box outlined in bronze (What is it? the agent proposes this — correct it here), and under it the category and room, a count of its detail shots (+1 detail shot) and the spoken field note (🎤); the item panel carries the editable note and the detail thumbnails. Until 2026-09-20 the row read Unnamed — shot 3, name it below, with nowhere below to name it; every row now has the box, named or not, so a name is typed or corrected in the list itself. Name it — by hand, or with Name N shots (below) — and it drops off the count. A detail shot is never a manifest line and is never numbered: two shots of one object are one line.
⚠⚠ The court list follows the CONTRACT now, not the service type (new 2026-09-21). Until this date the whole seven-item compliance list keyed on the service being Probate or Contested Probate, and that was wrong in both directions at once. An Estate Settlement administering a probate estate got no compliance list at all, whatever it had been contracted for — five desk boxes and nothing else. And a probate estate contracted at None, where the agreement says counsel does the whole inventory, was still told to verify date-of-death FMV on every line and attach the appraisals — two instructions nobody on the job could follow. Measured before it was changed: tier and matter type changed nothing across all twenty combinations on every one of the seven services. What decides each box now:
BoxRenders when
§733.604 inventory verified — date-of-death FMV on every linethe matter is Probate or Both and the tier is Inventory with values or Inventory + appraisals — we state the values
All professional appraisals attachedthe same matter test, and the appraisals are Havellin’s to arrange: the tier is Inventory + appraisals, or (since 2026-09-30) the approved estimate lists an appraiser — or the job actually has an appraiser on its roster, whatever the tier
Non-probate assets excludedthe same matter test, and the tier produces an inventory at all (anything but None)
Served · Filed within 60 days · Final accountingthe matter test alone — the court’s procedure happens whoever built the schedule
The PR’s sign-off box is gone (2026-10-03, P19). PR sign-off obtained on the final disposition summary was a tick that said a signature existed without the paper. The representative signs off on the Disposition Ledger, and its signed copy filed to Drive is the record; the trustee’s list (below) has no sign-off box either. A tick made under the old box stays on the record and is no longer drawn.

The same one-word fix reached the Job Plan: Personal Representative authority confirmed under Before Day 1 now follows the matter type too, so an Estate Settlement on a probate matter is finally asked it and a probate job recorded as a trust administration is not. Since 2026-10-03 a trust administration is asked its own twin instead, under a Trust gate heading: Certification of Trust (§736.1017) read — any limits on the trustee’s powers noted. A pour-over (Both) is asked both.
An unanswered matter type is not a trust matter, and a probate job created before the field existed keeps its checklist. A matter recorded on the job decides.
